MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2020-01-13 | CLIP_ID: 732 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 732

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
January 13, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case,  Bryan Cook  Ken Esquibel , Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna,  Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The 
pledge of allegiance was recited.   Mayor Orr recognized that Buck Holmes , Laramie County 
Commissioner and Kathy Emmons, City/County Health Department Director were in attendance.  
 
 Recognition of Rocky Case for his service as City Council President for 2019.  Mayor Orr 
provided a gavel plaque to Mr. Case and expressed appreciation for his services as Council 
President in 2019.  Mr. Case expressed appreciation for the opportunity to serve as president, 
reflected on the past year generally and thanked co-elected officials and the community.   
 
 Election of City Council President for 2020.   Mayor Orr announced that pursuant to Title 
2 of Cheyenne City Code the Mayor conducts the election for the office of City Council President 
but does not cast a vote. Mr. Luna nominated Dr. Rinne, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Dr. Rinne 
accepted the nomination.  There being no further nominations, Mr. Shanor moved to cease 
nominations, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion  carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body.  Motion to nominate and elect Dr. Rinne as City Counci l President for 2020 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  with the exception of Mr. Esquibel 
voting “no”.   
 
 Election of City Council Vice-President for 2020.  Mayor Orr announced that pursuant to 
Title 2 of Cheyenne City Code the Ma yor conducts the election for the office of City Council 
President but does not cast a vote.  Dr. Rinne moved to nominate Mr. White, seconded by Mr. 
Roybal.  There being no further nominations, Mr. Roybal moved to cease nominations, seconded 
by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body .  Mr. White 
accepted the nomination.  Motion to nominate and elect Mr. White as City Council Vice President 
for 2020 carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.   Mr.  Luna moved to approve , seconded by Mr . White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing.  
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on December 19, 2019.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
  
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, the North 
1/2 of Lot 4, Williams Subdivision, Laramie County, Wyoming (located northeast of the 
intersection of Marshall Road and Storey Boulevard).  Reporting for Public Services Committee, 
Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body.  
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from County LR Low -density Residential 
to City LR Low -density Residential for the North 1/2 of Lot 4, Williams Subdivision, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located northeast of the intersection of Marshall Road and Storey Boulevard).  
Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Dr. Rinne advised there 
was considerable discussion at the January 7, 2020 Public Services Committee meeting involving 
access issues to the area relating to emergency services.  Dr. Rinne further advised Charles Bloom, 
Planning & Development Director, will provide a proposal to rectify the access issues at the next 
Public Services C ommittee meeting  (January 22, 2020).  Motion car ried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments,

(Sponsors – Mr. Cook and Dr. Rinne)

[CA] Lease agreement between the City of Cheyenne and The Girl Scouts of Montana and

Agenda) carried (revenue to the City). (#7122) 
 
 Memorandum of Understanding between  Laramie County, Wyoming, and the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the acquisition of Lots 2, 3, and 4 of Frontier Park, a subdivision to the 
City of Cheyenne, consisting of 3.78 acres, situated in portions of Government Lots 1 and 2, NE 
¼ NE ¼ and SE ¼ NE ¼ of Section 25, Township 14 North, Range 67 West, of the 6th P.M., City 
of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming.  Tucker Fagan, City of Cheyenne-Laramie County Joint 
Powers Board Chairman, reported the Board subleases to C heyenne Frontier Days (CFD) and 
requested approval.  He explained the City is reclaiming Lots 2, 3, and 4, which is an area of land 
by Kennedy Dr. on the north side of park  and advised it was previously used for the Laramie 
County Fair but is no longer needed since the fair has moved entirely to the Archer Complex.  
Buck Holmes, Laramie County Commissioner, thanked the City for use of land in previous years 
and explained the County wishes to deed that property back to the City.  Mr. Holmes advised the 
Laramie County Commissioners have looked into removing the buildings but found it is not 
economically feasible, therefore, th ey are leaving them for CFD to use.  Tom H irsig, Cheyenne 
Frontier Days CEO, thanked the County for working with them over the years with the buildings 
and advised CFD does have some needs for carriage storage and can potentially use the buildings 
for CFD rodeo needs.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve, seconded 
by Mr. Luna.  Mr. White moved to amend by substitute dated January 6, 2020, seconded by Mr. 
Case.  Mr. White explained the amendment is to provide clarification of the property description 
on the title.  He explained there is an amendment for the MOU and an amendment for the affiliated 
contract, agenda item #18 (b), for the same purpose.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Hirsig confirmed maintenance of structures 
will be the responsibility of CFD.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. (#7123) 
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and A to Z Recreation LLC, for the Lincoln Park 
Play System Project.  (Development Impact Fees, Special Revenue Fund, CDBG Funds).  Motion

[CA] Agreement for services between the City of Cheyenne and the Church of St. Mary for

[CA] Sponsorship Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Charles Schwab, Independent

(Revenue to the City) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Volleyball Officiating Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Officials for Fun 
Club for adult City league volleyball officiating services during the Winter 2020 season

[CA] Memorandum of Understanding betw een the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, the City of 
Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities and the Laramie County Conservation District for the 
implementation of the design for the Crow Creek Revival Phase I that will improve the aquatic 
health of Crow Creek – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and AVI 
Professional Corporation, for the Capitol Basin 26th Street Storm Sewer Extension Design Project 
(Specific Purpose Option Tax Fund - 2012 Ballot Ove rages) – referred to Public Services 
Committee.  Dr. Rinne explained the item was heard under other items  at the January 7, 2020 
Public Services Committee, therefore, provisions of Title 2 of City code have been met.  Dr. Rinne 
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Tom Cobb, City Engineer, explained the agreement 
was delayed due to negotiations with the SLIB office and the necessary documents were not 
available for Council committee consideration until Tuesday, January 7, 2020.  Mr. Cobb advised 
the agreement should be expedited for protection of the Capitol Project and noted he will be asking 
for expedition of another affiliated contract.  Mayor Orr explained the Engineering Department is 
acting on a resolution adopted by the Governing Body, sponsored by Mr. Laybourn, to expedite 
the process.  Mr. Cobb explained the Request for Proposal (RFP) process was completed prior to 
him taking the City Engineer position and that 5 proposals were received, and 3 firms were  
interviewed with AVI being selected.  Comment was made on the process of bringing items up 
under other items for the agenda at standing Council committees and inquiry was made related to 
the necessity to do so with a large-scale project.  Concern was expressed regarding the expenditure 
and expediting the agreement without additional understanding about the protection of the S tate 
and the critical project for downtown .  Dr. Rinne advised there was discussion at the January 7, 
2020 Public Services Committee regarding necessity of bringing the item up under other items to 
expedite the project by 2 weeks, noting construction isn’t likely to begin until after Cheyenne 
Frontier Days.  Mr. Cobb explained the purpose is to expedite the design of the project so they can 
begin to understand what is underground  in an effort to be prepared for construction .  He noted 
projects of this nature often have unforeseen issues to address that often stall construction.  
Comments were made regarding how dangerous the TCE plume is and the importance of learning 
additional information prior to approval.  Additional comments w ere made in support of  
expediting the project and concern regarding proper protocol.  Mr. Shanor moved to refer the item 
to Finance Committee and to postpone to January 27, 2020 , seconded by Mr. Laybourn .  Kris 
Jones, City Clerk, upon inquiry,  explained the postponement would result in a 2 week delay.   
Discussion continued and included: support for the motion to refer the item to Finance Committee 
and postpone, selection process for the project, the importance of the project and sending the item 
to the appropriate committee.  Motion to refer to Finance Committee and postpone to January 27, 
  
2020 carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr, 
Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, and Mr. Roybal voting “no”. 
 
[CA]  Purchase and Sale Agreement between DSK Ranch, LLC, and the City of Cheyenne for

[CA] Amendment No. #1 to Contract # 7041 to Lease No. 8 -09466 between the State of

 Amendment #5 to Contract #2985 between the City of Cheyenne and the City of Cheyenne-
Laramie County Joint Powers Board, for the acquisi tion of Lots 2, 3, and 4 of Frontier Park, a

(#2985) 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -8-20 for furnishing One (1) Bomb Squad Robot, for the City of 
Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department.  (Homeland Security Grant, Project #2000563).  Motion

[CA] Consideration to purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and Titan Machinery Company,

 Appointments of Dennis Rafferty to the Housing and Community Development Advisory 
Council; Sally Sato the Mayor’s Council for Disabilities; Kathy Emmons and Lieutenant Eric Kent

 Voucher Report.  No comments were made.   
  
 Announcement of a Public Hearing to be held January 27, 2020 for a proposed sale of real 
property owned by the City of Cheyenne, described as Lots 1 through 12, Block 2, Roberts Place 
Addition, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, and more commonly referred to as 610 
West 7 th Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for the proposed price of $569,780.00, to the Laramie 
County School District Number One f or the essential future development of a new elementary 
school. 
 
 Announcement that due to the Martin Luther King Jr. Holiday on Monday, January 20, 
2020, regularly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instead be held in the Municipal 
Building, Room 104, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows: Finance Committee 
will meet Tuesday, January 21, 2020 at 11:00 a.m. and Public Services Committee will meet 
Wednesday, January 22, 2020 at Noon. 
 
 Under other business:  
 
 Donn Edmunds voiced concerns relating to  high-powered pickup truck headlights 
impeding the vision of other drivers and requested the governing body consider  taking action 
against the headlights. 
  
 Mr. Laybourn  provided comments relating to City goals for  fiscal year 2020 of the 
governing body and requested consideration to hold goal setting sessions  and additional work 
sessions noting the City of Casper posts their annual and on-going goals in City Hall for the benefit 
of the public.  Mr. Laybourn further commented on the upcoming legislative session and 
questioned what assistance, if any would the City have for important matters, noting  in the past 
there has been a lack of communication and joint understanding.  Mr. Esquibel advised there was 
a recent opportunity to meet with legislative delegations and there was a strong presence from the 
town of Albin . Mr. Esquibel advised he  looks forward to working with the governing body on 
legislative matters and goals.  Mayor Orr explained the City has been utilizing the Strat-Op process 
and is open to meeting with members of the governing body to discuss the process project.   
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:41 p.m.  
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2020-01-27 | CLIP_ID: 734 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 734

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
January 27, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark 
Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The 
pledge of allegiance was recited.  Mayor Orr recognized Boy Scout Troop 221 was in attendance. 
Mayor Orr asked for moment of silence for the 75th anniversary of the liberation of the Auschwitz-
Birkenau concentration camp.    
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Shanor, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Case and Mr. Cook,  agenda item #11 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Upon request by Mr.  Laybourn, and pursuant to support received by Dr. Rinne and Mr. Roybal,  
agenda item #15 (c) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Shanor, and 
pursuant to support received by Mr. Case and Mr. Cook, agenda item #15 (e) was removed from 
the Consent Agenda.   Mr.  Luna moved to approve  remaining items, seconded by Mr . White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne 
voting “no”.  
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing for a proposed sale of real property owned by the City 
of Cheyenne, described as Lots 1 through 12, Block 2, Roberts Place Addition, City of Cheyenne, 
Laramie County, Wyoming, and more commonly referred to as 610 West 7 th Street, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, for the proposed price of $569,780.00, to the Laramie County School District Number 
One for the essential future development of a new elementary school.  Teresa Moore, Community 
Recreation & Events D irector, provided a presentation  showing the property location which is 
adjacent to Cole Elementary school.  Ms. Moore reported Laramie County School District Number 
One (LCSD #1) would like to demolish the old school and construct a new elementary school in 
that location in the future.  Ms. Moore provided a detailed history of the City’s Neighborhood 
Facilities building which was previously occupied with city staff, noting the bui lding sustained 
water damage and clerical staff were relocated.  A portion of the building was secured for 
continuation of the gymnastics program.  She further noted that funding to remediate the building 
is not available and a location has yet to be ident ified for relocation of the gymnastics program.  
She advised staff is currently looking at proceeds of the sale and other funding sources to construct 
a new gymnasium facility to be built on property given to the City near the Ice and Events Center.  
Ms. Moore advised that an interim gymnastics program can temporarily remain on site or relocate 
at the Youth Activity Center.  Dennis Auker LCSD #1, reported on negotiations with the City to 
purchase the property and plans to construct a new elementary school have been on-going with the 
  
City for some time.  Mr. Auker explained that currently, the state has not yet approved the 
construction funding and advised other school district programs and activities may be held in the 
building.  Carla Gregorio, former principal at He bard Elementary, spoke in support of the 
agreement and expressed the need for a new elementary school in the area, noting she believes the 
school district would not displace the thriving City gymnastics program immediately and would 
allow them time to relocate.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing 
having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 13, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, the North 
1/2 of Lot 4, Williams Subdivision, Laramie County, Wyoming (locat ed northeast of the 
intersection of Marshall Road and Storey Boulevard).  Reporting for Public Services Committee, 
Mr. Shanor moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4292) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2 Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from County LR Low -density Residential 
to City LR Low -density Residential for the North 1/2 of Lot 4, Williams Subdivision, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located northeast of the intersection of Marshall Road and Storey Boulevard).  
Brandon Swain , applicant, complemented the City’s Planning & Development staff for their 
efforts.   Mr. Shanor moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Charles 
Bloom, Planning and Development Director, upon inquiry, stated staff worked with the applicant 
to create a development agreement to address access concerns.  Mr. Bloom advised the agreement 
addresses what type of development can be located in the a rea and will come to the governing 
body for approval at the February 10th City Council meeting.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#4293) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2, Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from LR Low Density Residential to NR -
2 Neighborhood Residential - Medium Density for the west half of Lot 2 and  all of Lot 3, 
Sunnyside Addition, 8th Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southeast corner of Dell Range 
Blvd. and Van Buren Ave.) .  Patrick Rudd, #1 Properties, representing the applicant, advised he 
was available for questions.  Nancy Mo nckton, adjacent landowner , voiced concerns with 
waterflow in the alley, noted parking concerns with any potential new development , and 
questioned trash receptacle placement and retrieval by the Sanitation Department.  Ms. Monckton 
advised she supports deve lopment of  low density residential (duplex) but nothing larger.  Mr. 
Shanor moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr.  White.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body.  
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapters 8.40, Food and Food Service 
Establishments, within Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
  
Wyoming, and Chapters 10.12, Administration and Enforcement, 10.16, Arrest and Citation 
Procedure, 10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking Generally, 10.60, Parking Limitations, 10.64, 
Loading and Unloading, 10.68, Critical Traffic and Parking Areas, within Title 10, Vehicles and 
Traffic, of the City of Cheyenne Municipal Code .  Mr. Shanor moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. Shanor moved to postpone to February 10, 2020, seconded 
by Mr. Luna.  Comments were made regarding parking program efforts for the past several years.  
Mr. Shanor advised he has received concerns and information from business owners in the 
downtown area and that the postponement would allow for additional public input and research on 
parking issues. The public was encouraged to contact the City Attorney’s office with any concerns 
or comments.  Motion to postpone carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
 
 RESOLUTION – Reappropriating the fiscal year 2020 budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W. S. §16-4-112 & §16-4-113.  Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded 
by Mr. Esquibel.  Mr. Shanor commented on concerns of using general fund reserves for a federal 
transit grant match through a mid-year reappropriation process.  Comments were made regarding 
revenue coming in below projections and current balance of general fund reserves.  Robin 
Lockman, City Treasurer, upon inquiry, reported this particular grant fund can be complicated to 
balance due to timing of expenditures and receipt of revenues, noting there was a period of time 
the fund wasn’t balanced in 2017.  Ms. Lockman explained the 50/50 grant match is a requirement 
and provided additional information relating to grant and match funding, noting the City cannot 
have a negative balance.  She further advised that going forward, it’s a 50/50 grant match of what 
the program costs for salaries, fuel, equipment, etc., and that Transit Division grant bus purchases 
require a 20% match.  Ms. Lockman reported revenues are currently below projection by 
approximately $800,000.00 and are showing a trend of slowing down, noting lottery proceeds have 
been less than last year.  Upon further inquiry, Ms. Lockman advised revenue from sale of property 
is considered as one-time funding and not annual revenue.  Motion to adopt carried. Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook voting “no”.  (#6035) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to execute State Historic 
Preservation Office Certified Local Government (CLG) Grant Agre ements for two 2020 CLG 
Grants (Board Training to attend two Historic Preservation conferences, and the Cheyenne Façade 
Improvement Program Project).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6036) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to  accept for ownership, 
development and maintenance, land from the Sweetgrass Land Co., LLC for the purpose of 
greenway and open space use pursuant to the recorded Sweetgrass 1 st Filing and Sweetgrass 2 nd 
Filing subdivisions – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
CA] RESOLUTION – Amending Resolution No. 5979 directing the availability of the 
production of Tchaikovsky’s The Nutcracker Ballet at the Cheyenne Civic Center – referred to 
Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
[CA] Sponsorship Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Charles Schwab, Independent 
Leader Sam Runyan, for a 7 year Stage Sponsorship for the Fridays on the Plaza (FOTP) events 
(Revenue to the City).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $126,000.00). 
(#7131) 
  
 
[CA] Volleyball Officiating Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Officials for Fun 
Club for adult City league volleyball officiating services during the Winter 2020 season 
(Recreation Special Revenue Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7132) 
 
 Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, the City of 
Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities and the Laramie County Conservation District for the 
implementation of the design for the Crow Creek Revival Phase I t hat will improve the aquatic 
health of Crow Creek.  Jeff Geyer, Laramie County Conservation District, expressed appreciation 
to the City Attorney’s office for reviewing the Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) in a timely 
manner and advised the MOU will allow 3 years of planning and designing to move forward with 
an anticipated spring implementation of the project.  Mr. Geyer advised permits  still need to be 
obtained prior to wor k being started.  D ena Egenhoff, BOPU, advised she is excited about the 
amount of progression that has taken place since the original agreement was approved and noted 
positive community engagement.  Ms. Egenhoff advised the MOU is a catalyst for Phase I and 
noted additional phases are currently unfunded, however, they will be seeking federal grant funds 
as a funding source.  Mr. Geyer added they have raised approximately $600,000.00 and following 
permitting, excavation of a large amount of material by Happy Jack Rd. and Westland Rd. will be 
completed to create a stream  which will expand the floodplain.  Mr. White moved to approve, 
seconded by Mr. Luna.  Comments were made related to the importance of the project for the 
community, the  history of Crow Creek , permitting process, removal of abandoned buildings, 
improvements to Crow Creek, future EUL development and the relocation of Cowboy Dodge .  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  (#7133) 
 
 Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and AVI 
Professional Corporation, for the Capitol Basin 26th Street Storm Sewer Extension Design Project 
(Specific Purpose Option Tax Fund - 2012 Ballot Overages) (Postponed from January 13, 2020).  
Michael O’Donnell, City Attorney, advised the proposed contract is for project design.  Mr. 
O’Donnell reported there were two options available:  Option #1 was to expedite the project which 
would include installing  a storm pipe in the ground prior to 2020 C heyenne Frontier Days  and 
Option #2 which would cost  significantly less doing design work in 2020  which would put the 
project out to bid this fall and begin work in Spring of 2021.  Mr. O’Donnell further advised that 
Governor Gordon opted to go with Option #2 as it was more fiscally responsible.  Clarification 
was given on design work to be completed by Fall of 2020.   Mr. White reported that the motion 
to approve at the January 21st Finance Committee meeting failed.  Mr. White moved to approve in 
an amount not to exceed $342,769.00, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Mr. White moved to amend by 
substitute dated January 21, 2020, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Upon inquiry, Mr. O’Donnell advised 
the design work is anticipated to be complete by August 2020, the project would then go out for 
bid in the Fall of 2020, and construction will commence in 2021 when weather allows and is 
anticipated to be completed late Summer of 2021.  Upon inquiry, Thomas Cobb, City Engineer, 
explained the worse case scenario would be if design work isn’t complete by October this year, 
completion of construction would be done no later than October 2021.  Mr. Cobb p rovided 
information on what will be included with the project.  Comments were made regarding the final 
step of Capitol Basin Project, review of 1985 flood issues, process of installing storm sewer, 
worsening weather events and patterns resulting in excessive flooding, other related difficult  
  
engineering projects , importance of the governing body’s oversite for extensive engineering 
projects and grant funding  availability relating to design changes to past engineering projects.    
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception 
of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  Main motion, as amended, carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  (#7134)   
 
  Purchase and Sale Agreement between DSK Ranch,  LLC, and the City of Cheyenne for 
real property described as a Portion of the East Half of the East Half (E½E½) of Section 35, 
Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, in the amount of 
$2,500,000.00 to be used as a future  City park in accordance with the terms of the 2017 1% 
Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax Ballot and Election (Specific Purpose Option Tax Fund - 2017 
Ballot).  Michael O’Donnell, City Attorney, provided additional property value documentation 
that included a land analysis prepared by Brendan Ames, Chief Economic Development Officer, 
and a 2017 Appraisal Report from Accuvalue, LLC.   Mr. O’Donnell advised he would be referring 
to this agreement as the “East Project” as it was on the 2012 Specific Purpose Tax ballot for the 
purpose of acquiring a track of land owned by DSK Ranch , LLC for  a park on the east side of 
Cheyenne.  He provided a p resentation that showed the location of the land that is proposed for 
purchase, description of wetland areas, location of proposed ball fields , and noted an existing 
tunnel is anticipated to be used as access to the greenway path.  He further advised there is a need 
for girl’s fast pitch softball fields in the community.  It was explained there are two tracts of land, 
one is the proposed purchase and  the adjacent land is to be obtained by L aramie County 
Conservation District which will not be developed , doubling the size of recreational amenity 
without increasing cost.  The tracts of land are connected by a tunnel.  Mr. O’Donnell provided 
detailed information regarding the previous appraisal in the amount of 1.1 million and explained 
that since that time, review o f fair property value has been researched.  He further explained that 
the original appraisal did not take into consideration that it would be used for recreation amenities 
and was appraised as bare ranch land . The new analysis is fair market value and take s in 
consideration properties that are similar in use .  Further comments were offered related to  
continued development around the proposed park land, existing water that could be used for water 
features, accessibility of land  and existing City utilities available on north and west portions of 
property.  Mr. O’Donnell explained the seller has reduced the asking price by $700,000.00 and has 
displayed impressive cooperation and patience relating to the sale of the property for the sake of 
community.  Brandan Ames, Chief Economic Development Office, provided detailed information 
relating to the fair market property valu ation document and confirmed the negotiated 
$2,500,000.00 price.   Dale Keizer, DSK Ranch, LLC, advised he has worked on the project with 
the City for the past 6.5 years, stating it is now up to governing body to  decide whether or not to 
purchase the property and voiced opposition to postponement.  Mr. Keizer, upon inquiry, reported 
he has been in the development business for 40 years and has owned the proposed property for 20 
years.  Cody Hess, Hess Development, spoke in support of the agreement for future growth and 
development in the area , noting he has worked with Mr. Keizer for several years .  Mr. White 
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Appreciation was expressed to Mr. Keizer and Mr. 
O’Donnell on their efforts relating to the project.  Concern was expressed regarding purchasing 
the property  based solely on an internal staff analysis of the p roperty and suggestion for a 
postponement was made for the purpose of obtaining a professional independent cost analysis.  
Mr. Shanor moved to postpone, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Comments were made in opposition of 
  
a postponement and support for the presented fair market value and negotiated purchase price , 
support of future greenway connectivity relating to area subdivisions and recreational opportunity.  
Upon inquiry, Mr. O’Donnell stated in his opinion, real estate professionals value land based on 
how the property currently exists.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Ames provided information on his 
professional background prior to his employment with the City which included experience of being 
a real estate agent.   He explained he has had preliminary conversations with real estate agents and 
appraisers who were reluctant to provide an appraisal for various reasons and noted it’s difficult 
to value property fo r a civic purpose as they generally place a value on land for its current use.   
Mr. Ames explained that appraisals and market analysis are similar and that an appraiser gets more 
into details in terms of measurements , etc.  Discussion continued on the foll owing topics:  
Obtaining a formal appraisal/market value, options on how to proceed,  growth of the community 
to the east, lack of and need of recreational amenities in that area, history of the project, possible 
alternatives, potential for the land purchase to provide multiple recreation opportunities and the 
current opportunity to create a legacy park.  Mr. Keizer responded to an inquiry and confirmed if 
the item were to be postponed, he would seek other options for the sale or development of the 
property.  Comments were made indicating the price of the property is in line with market value , 
noting that a realtor won’t take certain future plans into consideration and would only be looking 
at it in its current use.  Mayor Orr announced a brief recess at 7:44 p.m. to allow Mr. Ames time 
to place the Land Analysis on official City letterhead.  Mayor Orr reconvened the meeting at 7:55 
p.m.  Motion to postpone failed with Mr. Roybal and Mr. Shanor voting “yes” and Mr. Case, Mr. 
Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mayor Orr, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, and Mr. White voting “ no”.  Appreciation 
was expressed to  Mr. Keizer for his patience during the  sale and purchase process.  Motion to 
approved carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr.  
Shanor voting “no”.  (#7135) 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming and the City of Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities for the GIS Cooperative Budget 
Consolidation (City General Fund and County funds) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and the Laramie County

Creek and its tributaries rehabilitation project.  (1991-1994 1% Sales Tax per Resolution #4093 – 
funds moved in 2013 to the Specific Purpose Sales Tax Fund and proceeds from purchase and sale

[CA] Purchase and Sale Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Laramie County 
School District Number One, for real property described as Lots 1 through 12, Block 2, Roberts

610 West 7 th Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming, in the amount of $569,780.00, to be used as a future 
elementary school  (Revenue to the City) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Development agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Brandon Swain regarding fire 
access (Northwest of Marshall Road and Storey Boulevard) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
  
[CA] Letter of Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, and the United 
States Department of Justice, Drug Enforcement Administration, for locating and eradicating illicit 
cannabis – referred to Finance Committee. 
  
[CA] Consideration to purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and Titan Machinery Company, 
Gillette, WY, for the repair of Street and Alley Vacuum Truck Block #5889 (Fleet Maintenance).  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $46,760.86). 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-7-20 for furnishing As Needed Emergency Snow Control for the 
City of Cheyenne, Public Works Department (2015-2018 & 2019-2022 1% Sales Tax) – referred 
to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -10-20 for furnishing new computers and operating systems for 
the City of Cheyenne Information Technol ogy Division (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
 Voucher Report.  No comments were made.   
 
 Revenue Report.  No comments were made.  
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held February 10, 2020 at 6:00pm in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O'Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to  determine whether 
compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for a portion 
of the S½ of Section 35, and a portion of the SW¼ of Section 36, T14N, R67W Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located between Old Happy Jack Road and Happy Jack Road, west of I-25).   
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held February 10, 2020 at 6:00pm in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O'Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a restaurant liquor license 
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for C & Z LLC, d/b/a China Buffet, 1958 Dell Range 
Blvd., Suite D, Cheyenne, Wyoming.   
 
 Under other business:  
 
 Mr. Luna advised that he recently met with the City Attorney and inquired as to the current 
status of the demolition of the  house near the roundabout  located at 2247 E. 19 th Street.  Mr. 
O’Donnell advised the demolition process was delayed due to the required DEQ approval process 
involving contamination issues and is set to be demolished by June 30, 2020.     
 
 Mr. Roybal recognized the attendance of Boy Scouts Troop 221 and commented on his 
involvement with the Troop. 
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:02 p.m.  
 
 
  
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2020-02-10 | CLIP_ID: 735 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 735

  
 
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 10, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky 
Shanor and Jeff White .  Absent: Rocky Case and Bryan Cook.   Also present: Kris Jones, City 
Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Luna and Mr. Roybal, agenda item #16 (c) was removed from the Consent Agenda.   Mr. 
Luna moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body present.  
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 27, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a p ublic hearing  to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for a portion of the S½ of Section 35, 
and a portion of the SW¼ of Section 36, T14N, R67W Laramie County, Wyoming (located 
between Old Happy Jack Road and Happy Jack Road, west of I -25). Seth Lloyd, Planning and 
Development Department,  advised the Cit y has initiated the annexation, provided information 
relating to the property and reviewed requirements of and compliance with annexation conditions 
contained in state statutes .  Mr. Lloyd reported the affiliated annexation ordinance would be 
introduced at a later date.  There being no comments or input and purpose of the hearing having 
been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing for a restaurant liquor license application filed with the 
City Clerk’s Office for C & Z LLC, d/b/a China Buffet, 1958 Dell Range Blvd., Suite D, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming.  Upon inquiry, Kevin Christianson, representing C & Z LLC, explained the request is 
for a new license due to new ownership.  Mr. Christianson advised the current license will expire 
on March 31, 2020 and the new one will take effect the following day , April 1, 2020 due to the 
timing of annual license renewals and the new ownership.  Kris Jones, City Clerk, confirmed upon 
inquiry, employees are required to receive TIPS training in order to serve alcohol.  There being no 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr referred the item to the 
Finance Committee and closed the hearing. 
 
  
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2, Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from LR Low Density Residential to NR-
2 Neighborhood Residential - Medium Density for the west half of Lot 2 and all of Lot 3, 
Sunnyside Addition, 8th Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southeast corner of Dell Range 
Blvd. and Van Buren Ave.).  Sharman Straub, area land owner, reported she did not receive 
notification of the proposed zone change , noted objection  and stated the following concerns:  
multi-family development relating to small lot size, lack of current adequate drainage, roadways 
icing over during winter mon ths, questioned whether setbacks would be vacated, parking issues 
and limitations  and space restrictions involving landscaping requirements .  Ms. Straub voiced 
concerns relating to traffic access and egress relating to proposed roadway improvements on Van 
Buren Ave. Daniel Holbrook, property owner and applicant, reported he has met with some 
adjacent property owners related to concerns and advised he will meet all city code requirements 
related to parking, drainage  and setbacks.  He further noted he met with the traffic engineer on 
how the D ell Range expansion will impact this project  and reported it is being taken into 
consideration.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third 
and final readin g, seconded by Mr.  Shanor.  Upon inquiry related to adjacent property owner 
notification, Mr. Holbrook advised this is the first he had heard that a neighbor in the area hadn’t 
received notification. He explained he hired an engineering firm to send out required notifications 
and the firm provided information showing notice was sent to required area property owners.  He 
added he is willing to work with neighbors regarding their concerns.  Mr. Laybourn moved to 
postpone to February 24, 2020.  Charles Bloom, Planning & Development Director, reported as 
part of the proposed rezoning process, the applicant must send notice to properties within 300 ft.  
Mr. Bloom advised the City requires confirmation that all neighbors within 300 ft. receive notice 
and they believe everyone that was required to receive notice was properly noticed.  Mr. Bloom 
explained the property in question is within 300 ft.  Ms. Straub  upon inquiry advised they don’t 
reside at the property and that the current tenant is her daughter who reported she did not receive 
the notice.  Motion to postpone died due to lack of second.  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present with exception of Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne and Mr. White 
voting “no”.  (Clerk note -ordinances on third and final reading require a minimum of six (6) 
affirmative votes) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapters 8.40, Food and Food Service 
Establishments, within Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, and Chapters 10.12, Administration and Enforcement, 10.16, Arrest and Citation 
Procedure, 10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking Generally, 10.60, Parking Limitations, 10.64, 
Loading and Unloading, 10.68, Critical Traffic and Parking A reas, within Title 10, Vehicles and 
Traffic, of the City of Cheyenne Municipal Code  (Postponed from 01 -27-2020).  Mr. Roybal 
advised the item died at Public Services Committee due to lack of a motion.   Upon inquiry, Mr. 
Roybal explained the committee discussed division of the proposed amendments into multiple 
documents. Item died due to the lack of motion.   
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Chapter 5.52, Massage Establishments, within 
Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
  
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Chapters 8.40, Food and Food Service 
Establishments, within Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne,

of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsors – Dr. Rinne and Mr.

ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Chapters 10.12, Administration and

 [CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to  accept for ownership, 
development and maintenance, land from the Sweetgrass Land Co., LLC for the purpose of

[CA] RESOLUTION – Amending Resolution No. 5979 directing the availability of the

[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Alliance

[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Buffalo

County, Wyoming (located southeast of the intersection of Hilltop Av enue and  Bluff Place) – 
referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming and the City of Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities for the GIS Cooperative Budget 
Consolidation.  (City General Fund and County funds) .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
(#7142/7143) 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and the Laramie County 
Conservation District for allocation of funds for planning, design and implementation of Crow 
Creek and its tributaries rehabilitation project.  (1991-1994 1% Sales Tax per Resolution #4093 – 
funds moved in 2013 to the Specific Purpose Sales Tax Fund and proceeds from purchase and sale 
agreement of Lot 1,  Block 1, Pat Griffin Second Filing – funds located in the Specific Purpose 
Sales Tax Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $147,340.00). 
(#7144) 
 
  
 Purchase and Sale Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Laramie C ounty 
School District Number One, for real property described as Lots 1 through 12, Block 2, Roberts 
Place Addition, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, and more commonly known as 
610 West 7 th Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming, in the amount of $569,780.00,  to be used as a future 
elementary school  (Revenue to the City).  Mr. Luna moved to approve in an amount not to exceed 
$569,780.00, seconded by Mr. White.  Teresa Moore, Community Recreation & Events Director, 
upon inquiry  advised options are being explored relating to possible future relocation of the 
gymnastics program and noted they have had discussions with Laramie School District #1 
regarding leasing the current location until it is needed for development of a new elem entary 
school.  Robin Lockman, City Treasurer,  upon inquiry explained the amount of unassigned and 
unrestricted reserves is difficult to project and advised the amount changes month to month 
throughout the year.  Ms. Lockman provided additional accounting information involving revenue 
projections.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#7145) 
 
[CA] Development agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Brandon Swain regarding fire 
access (Northwest of Marshall Road and Storey Boulevard).   Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#7146) 
 
[CA] Letter of Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Police Depar tment, and the United 
States Department of Justice, Drug Enforcement Administration, for locating and eradicating illicit 
cannabis.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $75,000.00 – revenue 
to City). (#7147) 
 
[CA] Development Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Blue Federal Credit Union, 
Inc., for the redevelopment and access at the Pershing Boulevard/Converse Avenue/19 th Street 
Roundabout – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Audiomatrix Inc., for 
the City of Cheyenne Public, Educational, and Governmental (PEG) Access Channel

[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-7-20 for furnishing As Needed Emergency Snow Control for the

(per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from JTL Group, Inc. d/b/a Knife River, Cheyenne, 
in an amount not to exceed $200,000.00). 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -10-20 for furnishing new computers and operating systems for

amount not to exceed $49,691.00). 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-11-20 for furnishing one (1) new 2019 -2020 ¾ ton regular cab,

of Cheyenne, Parks Division  (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax)  -- referred to Finance Committee.  
  
 Voucher Report.  No comments were made.   
 
 Announcement was made that due to the President’s Day Holiday on Monday, February 
17, 2020, regularly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instead be held in the 
Municipal Building, Room 104, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows: Fin ance

 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held February 24, 2020 at 6:00pm in

 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held  February 24, 2020 at 6:00pm in

microbrewery, satellite distillery, winery and special malt beverage permits) filed with the City

 Under other business:  
 
 Upon inquiry, City Clerk Kris Jones clarified that ordinances on third and final reading 
require six (6) affirmative votes. 
 
There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:30 p.m.

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2020-02-24 | CLIP_ID: 736 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 736

  
 
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 24, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn (arrived at 6:03 p.m.), Mike 
Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also present: Kylie Soden, 
Deputy City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Mr. White, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Cook and Mr. Case, agenda item # 18 (a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.   Mr. 
Luna moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 10, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from the Special Meeting of the Governing Body on February 11, 2020.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing for a microbrewery liquor license application filed with 
the City Clerk’s Office for Black Tooth Brewing Company, LLC d/b/a/ Black Tooth Brewing, 520 
W. 19th St., Cheyenne, Wyoming.  There being no comments or input and purpose of the hearing 
having been met,  the item was referred  to the Finance Committee  and Mayor Orr  closed the 
hearing. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing for renewal of existing City liquor licenses (retail, 
restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar and grill  licenses, microbrewery, satellite distillery, winery 
and special malt beverage permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office .  There being no comments 
or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, the item was referred to the Finance 
Committee and Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter  5.52, Massage Establishments, 
within Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming.  Rachael Wetzel commented on the importance of the ordinance for women in the 
community, the need for the availability of breast massage services within city limits where there 
are regulations and spoke in support of the ordinance .  Mayor Orr expressed her appreciation to 
  
Ms. Wetzel for her involvem ent and for  bringing the issue forward for consideration.   City 
Attorney Michael O’Donnell advised he has been in contact with the Director of the Environmental 
Health Department related to the requirement of a medical referral for breast massage service 
patients and that they endorse the ordinance.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Case 
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr . Luna .  Mr. Case moved to amend by 
substitute dated February 24, 2020 which includes striking language “or distance learning school” 
in Section 5.52.105 (a)(2) and Section 5.52.105 (b)(2), seconded by Mr . Luna.  Motion to amend 
by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapters 8.40, Food and Food Service 
Establishments, within Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, and Chapters 10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking Generally, and 10.68, Critical 
Traffic and Parking Areas, within Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Case moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapters 10.12, Administration and 
Enforcement, 10.16, Arrest and Citation Procedure, 10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking 
Generally, 10.60, Parking Limitations, 10.64, Loading and Unloading, within Title 10, Vehicles 
and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Mr. Case moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Section 10.12.130, City Traffic Engineer, of 
Chapter 10.12, Administration and Enforcement, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the 
Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Case)

the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case)  
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Alliance 
Addition, 4th Filing, a replat of a portion of Lot 1, Block 1, Alliance Addition, Cheyenne, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located at the northwest corner of the intersection of Ridge Road and Jackson 
Street.  Mr. Case moved to adopt, seconded by Mr.  Luna. Comment was made o n the proposed 
land use worsening existing parking issues in the area.  Motion failed. Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, 
Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Shanor and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Luna, 
Mayor Orr, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Roybal.

County, Wyoming (located southeast of the intersection of Hilltop Avenue and Bluff Place).  Mr. 
Case moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. White.  Concern was expressed related to the lot size and 
  
the proposal of dividing the lot into 2 residential lots.  Motion failed due to a tie vote .  Voting 
“yes” – Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. 
Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Mayor Orr and Dr. Rinne. 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Creating a Revolving Real Property Special Revenue Fund to account 
for the acquisition and resale of real property of the City of Cheyenne – referred to Finance

[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne to approve and file the Wyoming 
Division of Victim Services Funding Application State Fiscal Year 2021 and 2022, July 1, 2020 
to June 30, 2022 – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Reappropriating the Fiscal Year 2020 Budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. §16-4-112 & §16-4-113 – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsors – Mr. Case, Mr. Luna, Mr. Roybal and Mr. Shanor) 
 
 Development Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Blue Federal Credit Union, 
Inc., for the redevelopment and access at the Pershing Boulevard/Converse Avenue/19 th Street 
Roundabout.  City Engineer Tom Cobb explained the agreement is for possible operational 
improvements and public access in perpetuity for the free right turn and north sidewalk.  Mr. White 
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. White moved to amend  by substitute Exhibits A, 
B and C that were distributed electronically on February 21, 2020 seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion 
to amend by substitute exhibits carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main 
motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#7149) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Audiomatrix Inc., for 
the City of Cheyenne Public, Educational, and Governmental (PEG) Access Channel 
Programming Equipment and Production Equipment Upgrade Project (2015 -2018 1% Sales Tax, 
Charter Equipment Funding Grant) .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an  amount not to 
exceed $74,537.65).  (#7150) 
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County School District Number 
One to provide transportation services for the Community Recreation & Events Summer Rec 
Camp Program (Recreation Program Fees) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-11-20 for furnishing one (1) new 2019 -2020 ¾ ton regular cab, 
full size pickup truck and one (1) new 2019-2020 ¾ ton ext. cab, full size pickup truck for the City 
of Cheyenne, Parks Division (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax).   Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried 
(accepted bid from Spradley Barr Motors, Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $67,703.00). 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-6-20 for furnishing a minimum of three (3) to a maximum of ten 
(10), lift -equipped transit vehicles, for the City of Cheyenne, Transit Division.  (FTA Transit 
Development Grant) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
  
[CA] Restaurant liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for C & Z LLC, 
d/b/a China Buffet, 1958 Dell Range Blvd., Suite D, Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried.  
 
 Voucher Report.  No comments were made. 
 
 Revenue Report.  No comments were made. 
 
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held March 9, 2020  at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O'Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a retail liquor license 
renewal application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Deep South LLC d/b/a Redwood Lounge, 
2105 E. Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
 Under other business:  
 
 Downtown Development Authority Executive Director Amber  Ash made comments on 
agenda item #9 which included the importance of food trucks in the community and concern for 
food trucks being able to park in front of businesses for long periods of time.  Ted Miazga, Parking 
Manager, Cheyenne Police Department, clarified the ordinance is to establish the option of food  
trucks being able to stay longer than 2 hours in front of a business if they are requested to be there. 
 
 Police Chief Brian Kozak provided an update on Calea accreditation the Police Department 
is looking to obtain.  He explained it is a 3 -year process and he is seeking governing body input 
before it is finalized.  Upon inquiry, Chief Kozak provided information on the accreditation 
program stating law enforcement has 181 different standards, many of which are high liability, 
and the program is to improve the delivery of public safety services  and best practices .  He 
explained the department is seeking 1 st level accreditation and the Wyoming Association of Risk 
Management will pay for the membership.  Upon further inquiry, Chief Kozak advised the 
accreditation may prove helpful when applying for grant funding.  Mr. Laybourn moved to approve 
and appreciate the Police Department’s accreditation application.  Concern was expressed related 
to approval of an item that hasn’t been previously reviewed.  Mayor Orr advised Chief Kozak sent 
an email with accreditation information and it can be re -sent to council members.  Motion failed 
due to lack of a second.  [Clerk’s note:  items that have not been reviewed by a standing committee 
are not eligible for consideration of approval by the governing body]    
 
 Mr. White reported the motion to adopt the following ordinance - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 
Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2, Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official 
Zoning Map of the Unified Development Code, changing the zoning classification from LR Low 
Density Residential to NR-2 Neighborhood Residential - Medium Density for the west half of Lot 
2 and all of Lot 3, Sunnyside Addition, 8 th Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the  southeast 
corner of Dell Range Blvd. and Van Buren Ave.)  failed on 3 rd and final reading at the February 
10, 2020 City Council meeting.  Having voted on the prevailing side and pursuant to Chapter 2.04 
of City Code, Mr. White moved to reconsider the vote taken on the ordinance, seconded by Mr. 
Cook.  Mr. White advised he reviewed additional information involving the ordinance related to 
the required notification process and requested support for the motion to reconsider.  Motion failed 
  
due to a tie vote.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Cook, Mayor Orr, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor and Mr. White.  
Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna and Dr. Rinne. 
  
 There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:28 p.m.  
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kylie Soden, Deputy City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  March 3, 2020

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
March 9, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Mike Luna, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  
Absent: Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne and Scott Roybal, Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  
The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.  Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.

Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from the Special  Meetings of the Governing Body on February 20, 25, 2020.  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing for a retail liquor license renewal application filed with 
the City Clerk’s Office for Deep South LLC d/b/a Redwood Lounge, 2105 E. Lincolnway, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming.  There being no comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been 
met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing and referred the application to Finance Committee. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Chapter  5.52, Massage Establishments, 
within Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Shanor moved to approve on third and 
final reading, seconded by Mr . Luna.  Motion  carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. (#4294) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Chapters 8.40, Food and Food Service 
Establishments, within Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, and Chapters 10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking Generally, and 10.68, Critical 
Traffic and Parking Areas, within Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by 
Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4295) 
 
 
  
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Chapters 10.12, Administration and 
Enforcement, 10.16, Arrest and Citation Procedure, 10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking 
Generally, 10.60, Parking Limitations, 10.64, Loading and Unloading, within Title 10, Vehicles 
and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Mr. Shanor moved to 
approve on third and final  reading, seconded by M r. Cook.  Mr. Shanor  moved to amend by 
substitute dated March 5, 2020  which includes City Attorney’s office technical corrections , 
seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mr. White commended Joe Chenchar, City Attorney’s office, for his work 
on the ordinance. Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing bod y 
present.  Main  motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  
present. (#4296) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 10.12.130, City Traffic Engineer, of 
Chapter 10.12, Administration and Enforcement, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the 
Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Mr. Shanor moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr.  Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Creating Section 2.04.200, Purchasing Policy, of 
Chapter 2.04, Mayor and City Council, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of the Municipal 
Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  TJ Bartt elbort, Purchasing Man ager, provided 
information on the ordinance  explaining it specifically provides guidelines for elected officials, 
department directors and employees who may be involved in the procurement process and  
addresses violations which may result in disciplinary actions. Mr. Barttelbort provided additional 
information on the Purchasing Policy explaining it is over 60 pages in length  and receives  
occasional revisions and updates.  Adopting just the forwarding of the policy allows future changes 
without needing to update the ordinance .  He noted policy c ontent has recently been revised and 
department directors have had a chance to review and submit comments/concerns.  Upon inquiry, 
Michael O’Donnell, City Attorney , advised Administrative Procedur e Act requirements don’t 
apply to the policy as it is not considered rules and regulations.  Discussion followed and included: 
statutory requirements, possible s ituations involving procurements by elected official s and 
purchasing policy violations.  Upon inquiry Mr. Barttelbort advised the purchasing policy has been 
reviewed by dep artment directors and can be provided to the governing body.  Reporting for 
Finance Committee, Mr. Luna moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr.  White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion 
of the South ½ of Section 36, T14N, R67W, 6th PM, Laramie County, Wyoming (located between

 [CA] RESOLUTION – Creating a Revolving Real Property Special Revenue Fund to account

 RESOLUTION – Reappropriating the Fiscal Year 2020 Budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. §16-4-112 & §16-4-113.  City Attorney Michael O’Donnell 
commented on the resolution noting some funding to be transferred to the Police Department from 
the Mayor’s discretionary account may no longer be available  due to an employee raise, funding 
for Police Department special events security has alre ady been accounted for  and the Police 
Department is unsure of what the funding is for .  Upon inquiry, City Treasurer Robin Lockman 
advised there hasn’t been a n employee raise given as of yet and the funding is still there.  She 
further explained an employe e raise can be paid for out of local meetings and/or professional 
development accounts because transfer of line item funding is allowed in d epartments and done 
without governing body approval and, if needed, a future reappropriation can be done to account 
for the raise.  Mr. Shanor provided information explaining in 2019 the Police Department charged 
$210,000.00 for special events security.  This  resolution attempts to create a line item to be used 
for putting money towards small special event security needs.  Comments were made related to 
how events are charged based on size and looking at improving the process for the future .  
Additional comments were made regarding the Police Department’s discretion of how funds are 
to be spent and what woul d determine equitable and fair charges for security services for special 
events.  Mr. Shanor moved to adopt, seconded by Mr.  Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present. (#6041) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne to accept as a gift a parcel of real 
property from JPK TR Cheyenne II, LLC – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsors – MR. 
Roybal and Mr. White) 
 
  
[CA] RESOLUTION – Supporting the City of Cheyenne Police Depart ment to become

[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the Ci ty of Cheyenne and Laramie County

 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
Appointments of Trent Carroll to the Board of Adjustment and Tyler McLachlan to the
