MEETING: City Council Meeting - Agenda Only | DATE: 2020-04-13 | CLIP_ID: 741 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 741

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
April 13, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:07 p.m. via electronic conference meeting.  Attending via electronic conference meeting were:  
MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete 
Laybourn, Mike Luna,  Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal,  Dicky Shanor and Jeff White .  Also 
attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Shanor and Mr. Case, agenda item #14 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Luna 
moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 23, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from the Special Meetings of the Governing Body on March 19, 2020.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
ORDINANCE – EMERGENCY READING – Amending Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic Liquor 
and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Municipal Code of the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, concerning the delivery of alcoholic liquor or malt beverages.  Mr. 
White moved to suspend the rules and hear this ordinance as an emergency ordinance pursuant to 
Wyoming State Statute 15-1-115(c), seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body.  Michael O’Donnell, City Attorney, provided details involving 
the proposed ordinance, noting it amends the existing ordinance and is restricted to a six month 
time frame.  Following comments regarding public comment availability relating to the electronic 
conference meeting, Mr. O’Donnell provided additional options for the public to provide 
comments/questions.  Comments were made regarding current revenue downfall for multiple types 
of businesses, compassion for business owners and employees during the Covid-19 Pandemic and 
mental health and substance abuse concerns related to the ordinance .  Upon inquiry, City Clerk 
Kris Jones explained that under the emergency ordinance procedure, three -quarters of the 
governing body must vote affirmatively in order for the ordinance to pass.   Mr. White moved to 
approve, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. (#4299) 
 
  
ORDINANCE – EMERGENCY READING – Establishing enforcement provisions for the 
Wyoming Statewide Public Health orders related to the Covid-19 Pandemic.  Mr. White moved to 
suspend the rules and hear this ordinance as an emergency ordinance pursuant to Wyoming State 
Statute 15-1-115(c), seconded by Mr. Laybourn.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body.  Michael O’Donnell, City Attorney, provided information relating to the 
proposed ordinance which would provide loca l authorities the ability to issue citations for 
violations under Municipal Code instead of under State Statute mandates, lowering the penalties.  
Mr. O’Donnell further explained that the intent is to encourage people to follow the recent orders 
put in place by the Governor regarding social distancing during the Covid-19 Pandemic.  Members 
of the public voiced concerns relating to the following: enforcement, constitution al rights, 
necessity, future recreational opportunities, public testing and community impacts from the Covid-
19 Pandemic, Health Department recommendations  and criminal versus civil convictions.  Mr. 
White moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Laybourn .  Mr. White moved to amend by substitute 
dated April 13, 2020, seconded by Mr. Laybourn.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook voting “no”.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend by 
replacing Section 9 to read as follows: “This ordinance shall cease to be effective upon any Order, 
or aspect of any Order, when the Order or aspect ceases to be in effect” , seconded by Dr. Rinne.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exceptio n of Mr. 
Cook voting “no”.  Additional discussion topics included potential for a “stay at home order”, civil 
versus criminal convictions and community pressure for more and less restrictions.  Upon inquiry, 
Chief of Police Brian Kozak advised currently th e Police Department refers to the Health 
Department to address complaints related to social gatherings and they have not yet had to enforce 
state law.  He further stated the Police Department remains neutral towards the ordinance.   Main 
motion as twice amended failed due to a tie vote.  Voting “yes”  – Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, 
Mayor Orr, Dr. Rinne, Mr. White. Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Mr. Roybal, and 
Mr. Shanor.      
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion 
of the South ½ of Section 36, T14N, R67W, 6th PM, Laramie County, Wyoming (located between 
Westland Road and I-25, adjacent to Crow Creek).  Mark Christensen, Planning and Development, 
advised he was available for any questions.  Mr. Case moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr.  Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
(#4300) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County LI (Light Industrial) to P (Public 
District) for a tract of land generally located west of Westland Road at Crow Cree k.  Mark 
Christensen, Planning and Development, explained the application accompanie s the previous 
agenda item (annexation request) and is for a zoning map amendment for 4 City parcels.  Mr. Case 
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr.  Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4301) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2, Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from LI Light Industrial to MUB Mixed-
  
Use Business Emphasis for Lots 5, 6, 7, and 8, Block 271, Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming 
(located at the northwest corner of the intersection of West 20 th Street and Bent Avenue) . Seth 
Lloyd, Planning and Development , provided brief comments on the  proposed zone change area.   
Darin and Misha Westby spoke in support of the ordinance and expressed appreciation to the 
governing body and Planning and Development staff. Mr. Case moved to approve on third and 
final reading, seconded by Mr.  Roybal.  Motion  carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. (#4302) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6 and 2.4.1 of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (UDC), amending Section 5.1.5.a -Detached Dwelling (“Single-
Family”) Lot Types and Building Standards to reduce the required frontage of Detached Dwelling 
(”Single-Family”) lots for DD7: Detached Dwelling/Small Lot Type from 48 feet to 40 feet and 
from 60 feet to 45 feet on corner lots .  Mark Christensen, Planning and Development, provided 
brief comments on the ordinance noting the reduction of required frontage of detached dwelling 
and corner lots.  Mr. Case moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr.  Luna.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4303) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee 
Schedule. Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, summarized the proposed fee schedule and 
explained they propose to move forward with the Integrated Solid Waste Management Plan update.  
Ms. Nemecek added that with the current pandemic and associated quarantine, commercial 
businesses can opt to alter or suspend their pick-up service due to less accrued waste and residents 
can order an additional waste receptacle due to increased waste volume.  She noted the proposal 
includes moving forward with the $8 million landfill expansion project recommended as a part of 
the Integrated Solid Waste Management Plan of which the increased expenditure will result in an 
increase of $1.24 million transfer to the general fund.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Nemecek explained an 
email sent to the governing body with proposed increase rates represent the same annual 
recommended increases in the ordinance  and the proposed $1.24 million transfer to the general 
fund is approximately half a million dollars more than last year.  Upon further inquiry related to a 
higher increase to some services, Ms. Nemecek advised the Integrated Solid Waste consultant 
reviewed services and found that the City’s cost to provide the services was lower than what has 
been charged for those services.  She further noted that adjustments were made so that the service 
charge is the same as what it costs to provide the service.  Mr. Luna moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body with the exception of Mr. Luna voting “no”. 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne to accept as a gift a parcel of real 
property from JPK TR Cheyenne II, LLC. Mr. Luna moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Case.  Dr. 
Rinne provided information on the parcel explaining it will be beneficial for the City to own it as 
it is adjacent to the Ice and Events Center.  He further explained this will be viable to future buyers, 
will assist in long term development of the property and abatement of existing structures will have 
to take place eventually.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with 
the exception of Mr. Cook voting “no”. (#6045)  
 
  
[CA] RESOLUTION – Approving the City of Cheyenne’s Analysis of Impediments to Fair 
Housing Choice Prepared by the Cheyenne Housing and Community Development Office – 
referred to Public Services Committee.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#6046) 
 
RESOLUTION – In appreciation of State and L ocal Leaders for their exceptional efforts 
during the Covid-19 Crisis.  Mr. Case advised this item was reviewed under other agenda items at 
the April 7, 2020 Public Services Committee meeting and provisions of City Code Title 2 had been 
met.  Mr. Case move d to adopt, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#6047) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Cheyenne Police Department to apply for Wyoming 
Association of Sheriffs and Chiefs of Police/Wyoming Department  of Transportation, Highway 
Safety Program, Selective Traffic Enforcement Grant Funding (Highway Safety Selective Traffic 
Enforcement Grant) (Sponsor – Mr. Luna) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Cheyenne Police Department to apply for Wyoming 
Department of Transportation, Highway Safety Program, Grant Funding for the City’s Mobile 
D.U.I. Command Post (Highway Safety Mobile Command Grant) (Sponsor – Mr. Luna) – referred 
to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Saddle 
Ridge, 15 th Filing, a replat of all of Lots 2 -10, Block 1, Saddle Ridge, 13 th Filing, Cheyenne, 
Laramie County, Wyoming (located northwest of the intersection of Saw Buck Road and Red

[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Westby 
Edge, a replat of Lots, 5, 6, 7 and 8, Block 271, Original City, Cheyenne, Laramie County , 
Wyoming (located at the northwest corner of W. 20 th Street and Bent Avenue) (Sponsor – Mr. 
Case) – referred to Public Services Committee. 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Granting an exception to the ‘201 Agreement’ for the owner of the 
property located 7511 Tate Road to allow repair or replacement of an on-site septic system located 
within Laramie County (Sponsor – Mr. Case) – referred to Public Services Committee. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement with Woolpert, Inc., for Civil Engineering and Design 
Services for the F.E. Warren AFB Enhanced Use Lease Project on Happy Jack Road.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $394,560.00) (#7162) 
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Laramie County Sheriff’s Department and 
Laramie County, Wyoming, for housing City prisoners at the Laramie County Detention Center  
(General Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Juvenile Housing Agreement between the City of Cheyenne  and Laramie County for the

Complex  (General Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
  
 
[CA] Grant agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Youth Alternatives, and t he Corporation 
for National and Community Service, for the FY 2020 Foster Grandparent Program (FGP) 
Replacement Sponsor Program  (Foster Grandparent Grant) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne , Laramie County , and C hristensen’s Inc., 
granting an access easement for use and maintenance of an access road and drainage conveyance 
across land owned by the City (revenue to the City) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne , Laramie Coun ty, and Christensen’s Inc., for 
driveway and drainage maintenance of an access road and drainage conveyance across land owned 
by the City – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 [CA] Consideration of Modification # 1 to Contract # 6933 between the City of Cheyenne and 
Vortex International Aquatic Play Solutions to extend the completion date.   Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. (#6933) 
 
[CA] Modification #1 to Contract #7136 City of Cheyenne and State of Wyoming Department 
State Parks and Cultural Resource s, for grant agreement: Board and Staff Training: 2020 Saving 
Places Conference (SHPO Federal Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (revenue to the 
City in an amount not to exceed $833.88). (#7136)    
 
[CA] Consideration of Modification #3 to Contract #6892 between Noel Griffith Jr. & Associates 
Architects for Additional Design Services and Historical Consulting for finish of the second floor 
of the Cheyenne Municipal Court Project (Specific Purpose Sales & Use Tax) – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #2 to Contract #7082 between the City of Cheyenne and Scotch 
Boy, Inc. for the Irrigation Controllers and Equipment Upgrade Project – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-8-20 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Recycled 
Materials, LLC, for the 101 Columbus Drive Demolition Project  (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax ).  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from Recycled Materials, LLC, Casper, WY, 
in an amount not to exceed $38,153.00). (#7163)  
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-15-20 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Pine Bluffs 
Gravel & Excavating for furnishing Compost Facility Waste Hauling (Solid Waste Fund) – 
referred to Finance Committee.    
 
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 Under other business: 
 
  
 Mr. Esquibel inquired into the possibility of opening softball fields for practice with similar 
social distancing restrictions that the golf course currently has in place.  Mayor Orr explained that 
violations of the Governor’s order related to social distancing were occurring at the softball fields, 
noting teams can’t gather at the fields. 
 
 There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:15 p.m.  
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting - Agenda Only | DATE: 2020-04-27 | CLIP_ID: 743 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 743

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
April 27, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:07 p.m. via electronic conference meeting .  Attending electronic conference meeting  were:  
MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete 
Laybourn, Mike Luna,  Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal,  Dicky Shanor and Jeff White .  Also 
attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Roybal, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Shanor and Mr. Cook, agenda items #17(d) and #17(e) were removed from the 
Consent Agenda.  Mr. White moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   
 
Comments were made related to public comment on agenda items.  Michael Skinner 
provided information on the raising hand function using WebEx.   
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on April 13, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee 
Schedule. Vicki Nemecek, Director of Public Works, advised she was available for any questions 
and recommended approval of the ordinance. Mr. Luna confirmed he continues to oppose increases 
to the solid waste fees.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Luna moved to approve  on third 
and final reading, seconded by Mr.  Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Luna voting “no”. (#4304) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Chapter 10.56, Handicapped Parking Spaces, 
within Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
(Sponsor – Mr. White) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Approving and establishing an amendment  to the 
Temporary Permit Process contained in Title 5 of City Code (Sponsors – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. 
Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor & Mr. White) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Cheyenne Police Department to apply for Wyoming 
Association of Sheriffs and Chiefs of Police/Wyoming Department of Transportation, Highway 
  
Safety Program, Selective Traffic Enforcement Grant Funding (Highway Safety Selective Traffic 
Enforcement Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6048) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Cheyenne Police Department to apply for Wyoming 
Department of Transportation, Highway Safety Program, Grant Funding for the City’s Mobile 
D.U.I. Command Post (Highway Safety Mobile Command Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#6049) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Saddle 
Ridge, 15 th Filing, a replat of all of Lots 2 -10, Block 1, Saddle Ridge, 13 th Filing, Cheyenne, 
Laramie County, Wyoming (located northwest of the intersection of Saw Buck Road and Red 
Feather Trail).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6050) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Westby 
Edge, a replat of Lots, 5, 6, 7 and 8, Block 271, Original City, Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located at the northwest corner of W. 20 th Street and Bent Avenue) .  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. (#6051) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Granting an exception to  the ‘201 Agreement’ for the owner of the 
property located 7511 Tate Road to allow repair or replacement of an on-site septic system located 
within Laramie County.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6052) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Providing for Public Safety Services at Cheyenne Frontier Days and 
Compensation to the City.  Tom Hirsig, CEO of CFD, voiced appreciation to those involved with 
preparation and compromising efforts.  Reporting for the Public Services Committee, Mr. Case 
advised this item was reviewed under other items  for the agenda at the April 21, 2020 Public 
Services Committee meeting, therefore  provisions of Title 2 of City Code have been met .  Mr.  
Case move to adopt, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. Case moved to amend by substitute dated April 
27, 2020, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Mr. Case advised the substitute clarifies language related to the 
technicalities as to what the Memorandum of Understanding ( MOU) will accomplish.  Chief  of 
Police Brian Kozak expressed support for the proposed substitute which allows for staffing  
considerations, provides  collaboration with C heyenne Frontier Days (C FD) and protects City 
liability with the annual event.  Chief Kozak expressed appreciation to Tom Hirsig and Pat Crank 
for working with the City on the details of the MOU.  Mr. H irsig advised he supports the 
substitution.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body.  Mr. Hirsig, upon inquiry, reported CFD has not made a final determination whether the 
annual CFD event will take place this year due to the Covid -19 pandemic.  Mr. Case voiced 
opposition to the inquiry of whether CFD will occur this year as he believes the comments weren’t 
germane to the MOU topic.  Mayor Orr overruled Mr. Case’s comment.  Mr. Hirsig further advised 
he doesn’t have an answer as to when an announcement wou ld take place, not ing CFD will 
continue to work with the City, County and State Health departments for guidance.  Upon further 
inquiry, Mr. Hirsig advised he anticipates a decision will be made by or during the first week of 
June.   Concern was expressed relating to the process utilized to consider the Resolution and MOU 
noting the item did not go before the Finance Committee and was brought up under Public Services 
Committee other items for the agenda, limit ing the public’s ability to participate and comment.  
  
Mr. Case advised the issue has been in need of resolution for months whether CFD happens this 
year or not, noting the MOU is for a 5 year term.  Comments were made regarding established 
processes and lack of communication between Council members.  Upo n inquiry, Michael 
O’Donnell, City Attorney, advised the practice is not ideal in terms of bi nding future governing 
bodies relating to financial decisions.  Mr. O’Donnell reported it is acceptable for future 
amendments and reconsideration by future governing bodies, noting the proposed Resolution and 
MOU contain language which allows CFD or the City to dissolve the agreement.  Upon inquiry, 
Mayor Orr advised budgetary decisions are made by recommendations of the City Treasurer which 
are either approved or denied by the governing body.  Comments were voiced that not all members 
of the governing body and City  staff members received notification of the proposal  in the 
Resolution or MOU.  Mr. Case moved to call for the question, seconded by M r. Luna.  Upon 
inquiry, Kris Jones, City Clerk, advised a motion to call for the question  is non-debatable and 
requires a 2/3 affirmative vote of those present.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr and Mr. Shanor voting “no”.  Main motion 
as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. 
Laybourn and Mayor Orr voting “no”.  (Res. #6053/MOU #7169) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – In appreciation of the teachers of L aramie County School District 
Number One and Laramie County Community College for their exceptional efforts during the 
Covid-19 Pandemic (Sponsor – Mr. Laybourn) – referred to Public Services Committee. 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Willow 
Heights, a replat of Lot 14, Block 5, Homestead Addition, First Filing Replat, Cheyenne, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located south of Homestead Avenue, between Henderson Drive and Hanson 
Street) (Sponsor – Mr. Case) – referred to Public Services Committee.    
 
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Laramie County Sheriff’s Department and 
Laramie County, Wyoming, for housing City prisoners at the Laramie County Detention Center  
(General Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (# 7165) 
 
[CA] Juvenile Housing Agreement between the City of Cheyenne  and Laramie County for the 
housing of juvenile detainees in the Laramie County Juvenile Services Center located at the Archer 
Complex (General Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7166) 
 
[CA] Grant agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Youth Alternatives, and the Corporation 
for National and Community Service, for the FY 2020 Foster Grandparent Program (FGP) 
Replacement Sponsor Program  (Foster Grandparent Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried 
(revenue to the City in the amount of $318,375.00). (#7167) 
 
 Agreement between the City of Cheyenne , Laramie County , and Christensen’s Inc., 
granting an access easement for use and maintenance of an access road and drainage conveyance 
across land owned by the City (revenue to the City) .  Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by 
Mr. White.  Mr. Luna moved to postpone  to May 5, 2020, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion to 
postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  (Clerk’s note: clarification 
of postponed date on following agenda item)   
  
 
 Agreement between the City of Cheyenne , Laramie County , and Christensen’s Inc., for 
driveway and drainage maintenance of an access road and drainage conveyance across land owned 
by the City. Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. Luna moved to postpone 
to May 11, 2020, seconded by Mr. Cook. City Clerk Kris Jones clarified both agenda items #17(d) 
and #17(e) will be postponed to May 11th.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. 
 
[CA] Development Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Leader Custom Homes, LLC, 
for regrading, installation and construction of sidewalks, utilities, drainage features and 
landscaping on City property in conjunction with development of the Willow Heights Subdivision 
– referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Maintenance Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Leader Custom Homes, LLC, 
for maintenance of improvements made upon land owned by the City in the area of the Willow 
Heights Subdivision – referred to Finance Committee.  
 
[CA] Consideration of Modification #3 to Contract #6892 between Noel Griffith Jr. & Associates 
Architects for Additional Design Services and Historical Consulting for finish of the second floor 
of the Cheyenne Municipal Court Project  (Specific Purpose Sales & Use Tax ).  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $16,120.00). (#6892) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #2 to Contract #7082 between the City of Cheyenne and Scotch 
Boy, Inc. for the Irrigation Controllers and Equipment Upgrade Project .  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. (#7082) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to  Contract #6798 between the City of Cheyenne and Trihydro 
Corporation, Laramie, Wyoming, for Happy Jack Landfill Environmental Monitoring Services 
(Solid Waste Fund) -- referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Extension Agreement to the Community Counseling and Interventio n Services 
Agreement (City Contract No. 6266) between the City of Cheyenne Youth Alternatives Division 
and Laramie County School District Number One (revenue to the City) – referred to Finance 
Committee.    
 
 [CA] Consideration of Bid #E-15-20 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Pine Bluffs 
Gravel & Excavating for furnishing Compost Facility Waste Hauling (Solid Waste Fund).  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from Pine Bluffs Gravel & Excavating in an amount 
not to exceed $158,597.13). (#7168)    
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-9-20 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and 4 Quarters 
Excavation & Development, for the Henderson Greenway Connector Project (Specific Purpose 
Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
  
 No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented. 
 
 Announcement of Public Hearing was made to be held May 11, 2020 at 6:00 p.m. via 
electronic conference meeting, in Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a transfer of retail liquor license 
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Ace Majestic, Inc. d/b/a Redwood Lounge and 
Package Liquors, 2105 E. Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held by Deep South 
LLC d/b/a Redwood Lounge) 
 
 Under other business: 
 
 Mr. Luna requested announcement of the following items – 
 ORDINANCE - approving and establishing amendments to various portions of Title 6 of 
City Code to redefine animal owner and amend fee structure – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 RESOLUTION - recommending a salary reduction for the City’s Governing Bod y – 
referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Mayor Orr commented on positive notes and comments she has received from the 
community recently in appreciation of the Governing Body and City employees. 
 
 There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was

        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting - Agenda Only | DATE: 2020-05-11 | CLIP_ID: 746 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 746

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
May 11, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:07 p.m. via electronic conference meeting.  Attending electronic conference meeting  were:  
MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete 
Laybourn, Mike Luna,  Dr. Mark Rinne , Scott Roybal,  Dicky Shanor and Jeff White .  Also 
attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk.    
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.  Upon request by Mr.  Laybourn, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Shanor and Mr. Roybal, agenda items #22 (c) and #22 (d) were removed from the 
Consent Agenda.  Mr. Luna moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.   Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   
 
[CA] Minutes from Regul ar Meeting of the Governing Body on April 27, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing for transfer of retail liquor license application filed with 
the City Clerk’s Office for Ace Majestic, Inc. d/b/a Redwood Lounge and Package Liquors, 2105 
E. Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held by Deep South LLC d/b/a Redwood 
Lounge).  There being no comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor 
Orr closed the hearing and referred the application to the Finance Committee. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter 10.56, Handicapped Parking Spaces, 
within Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
(Sponsor – Mr. White).  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Luna moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr.  Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the  governing 
body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Approving and establishing an amendment  to the 
Temporary Permit Process contained in Title 5 of City Cod e.  Mr. Luna moved to approve on 
second reading, seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Mr. Luna moved to amend by substitute dated May 5, 
2020, seconded by Mr.  Roybal.  Mr. Shanor explained the  proposed substitute reflects changes 
agreed upon between the City and Cheyenne Frontier Days.   Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  Main motion as 
amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  with the 
exception of Mr. Laybourn “no”. 
 
  
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Approving and establishing amendments to va rious 
portions of Title 6 of City Code to redefine animal owner and amend fee structure .  Mr. Luna 
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr.  Cook.  Comments were made regarding 
having additional communication between the City and the Animal Shelter relating to their annual 
budget.  Mr. Shanor explained the ordinance  addresses enforcement, defines animal ownership 
and raises certain fees and reported representatives from the Animal Shelter advised members of 
the Finance Committee that they support the proposed amendments.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Approving and establishing revised wat er and sewer 
rates, tap and system development fees, administrative fees, service charges and penalties, and 
septic and commercial waste dump rates, as recommended by the Board of Public Utilities – 
referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Laybourn) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming for various purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing 
the amount of general and special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said 
appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2020 and ending June 30, 2021 – referred 
to Committee of the Whole. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Sections 1.4.3, 2.1.1, 2.1.2, and 4.2.5 of the 
Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to define and clarify the purpose 
of remainder lots – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Section 2.2.6 of the Unified Development 
Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to modify time limits associated with temporary uses – 
referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Sections of Article 2 of the Unified 
Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to modify posted notice requirements for 
site plans and administrative approvals, clarify procedures for modification of site plans, modify 
authority to initiate text amendments, and modify criteria for variance approval – referred to Public 
Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
 RESOLUTION – In appreciation of the teachers of Laramie County School District 
Number One and Laramie County Community College for their exceptional efforts during the 
Covid-19 Pandemic .  Reporting for Public Services C ommittee, Mr. Case moved to adopt, 
seconded by Mr.  Luna.  Mr. Laybourn advised the proposed substitute includes the addition of 
other leaders and community members who have provided outstanding education al services 
relating to the current Covid -19 Pandemic for the youth of our community.  Mr. Case moved to 
amend by substitute, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body.  Main motion as amended by substitute carr ied.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body. (#6054) 
 
  
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Willow 
Heights, a replat of Lot 14, Block 5, Homestead Addition, First Filing Replat, Cheyenne, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located south of Homestead Avenue, between Henderson Drive and Hanson  
Street) (Sponsor – Mr. Case).  Mr. Case moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. Case moved 
to amend by revised final plat map dated May 4, 2020, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. Mr. Case moved to postpone to May 26, 2020, 
seconded by Mr. Shanor. Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. 
 
 RESOLUTION - Recommending a salary reduction for the City’s Governing Body .  Mr. 
Luna moved to adopt, seconded by Mr.  White.  Comments were made in regard to the proposed 
resolution relating to: process involving communication between members of the governing body, 
questions involving constitutionality, lack of City Attorney’s office support, general support of 
proposed amendments made at the May 4, 2020 Finance Committee, and confirmation that the 
proposal affects all members of the City Council.  Mr. Luna moved to amend by substitute authored 
by Councilman Shanor which revised the Now Therefore, Be It Resolved clause by striking #1 
and replacing it with: 1. The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne believes it is important for 
all members of the Governing Body to lead by example and incur a reduction in pay commensurate 
with the city department global percentage cut of 13% proposed in Mayor Orr’s FY 2021 budget 
proposal, and by adding a new Section #2 in the Now, Therefore, Be It Resolved clause to read as 
follows: 2.  All members of the Governing Body are encouraged to remit 13% of their salaries to 
the general fund each month of FY 2021 fiscal year , seconded by Mr.  Shanor.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Mr. Luna moved to amend by replacing 
“Governing Body” with “City Council”, removing the Mayor, seconded by Mr.  Cook.  Moti on 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Mr. Luna moved to amend the Now 
Therefore, Be It Resolved clause, Section 2, to read as follows: “All members of the City Council 
are encouraged to remit 13% of their salaries which will sunset on December 31, 2020”, seconded 
by Mr. Shanor.  Motion to amend carrie d with all members of the governing body voting “yes” .  
Mr. Luna moved to amend the Now Therefore, Be It Resolved clause, Section 2, by replacing 
“general fund” with “Forward Greater Cheyenne Emergency Relief Fund”, seconded by Mr.  
Shanor.  Mayor Orr, upon inquiry, advised that due to the City’s current payroll process, the City 
will not be making the transfer of funds and that members of the City Council wanting to contribute 
to the Forward Greater Cheyenne Emergency Relief Fund are individually responsible to make 
those contributions.  Motion to amend carried with  all members of the governing body voting 
“yes”.  Additional comments were made on the following topics:  amendment s made, 
constitutionality of resolution, policy, procedure and communication concerns, efforts in sharing 
a message that the governing body shares the community’s concerns and requests for increased 
transparency.  Main motion, as amended four times, carried with all members of the governing 
body voting “yes” with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  (#6055)

 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 Announcement was made that due to the Memorial Day holiday and City offices being 
closed on Monday, May 25, 2020, the regularly scheduled City Council  meeting will instead be 
held on Tuesday, May 26, 2020 at 6:00 p.m. via electronic conference meeting. 
 
 Under other business: 
 
 Mayor Orr commented on challenges o f present electronic conference meetings and 
advised she will be in contact with the City/County Health Department relating to having meetings 
of the governing body in the Civic Center which could allow for social distancing.  She reported 
the Wyoming State Legislature will face  similar challenges later this week when they convene 
during a special session.   
 
 There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:01 p.m.  
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting - Agenda Only | DATE: 2020-05-26 | CLIP_ID: 748 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 748

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
May 26, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:07 p.m. via electronic conference meeting.  Attending electronic conference meeting  were:  
MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook,  Pete Laybourn, 
Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Absent:  Ken Esquibel.  
Also attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk.    
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Mr. Luna, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Case and Mr. Cook, agenda item #29 (c) was removed from the Consent Agenda. Upon 
request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook and Mr. White, agenda 
item #18 was removed from the Consent Agenda.   Mr. White moved to approve remaining items, 
seconded by Mr . Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing  body 
present.   
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on May 11, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on May 13, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Chapter 10.56, Handicapped Parking Spaces, 
within Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  
Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Luna moved to approve on third and reading, seconded by 
Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#4305)  
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Approving and establishing an amendment  to the 
Temporary Permit Process contained in Title 5 of City Code.  Mr. Luna moved to approve on third 
and final reading, seconded by Mr.  White.  Motion  carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. [Clerk’s note: no verbal 
vote from Mr. Shanor was recorded.]  (#4306)  
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Approving and establishing amendments to various 
portions of Title 6 of City Code to redefine animal owner and amend fee structure .  Mr. Luna 
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr . White.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#4307)  
 
  
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Approving and establishing revised water and sewer 
rates, tap and system development fees, administrative fees, service charges and penalties, and 
septic and commercial waste dump rates, as recommended by the Board of Public Utilities .  Mr.

– all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Cook voting “no”.   
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne,

the amount of general and special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said

carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  present.  Dr. Rinne advised the next 
Committee of the Whole meeting for this agenda item will be held Wednesday June 10, 2020 at 
6:00 p.m. via electronic conference meeting.     
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Sections 1.4.3, 2.1.1, 2.1.2, and 4.2.5 of the

of remainder lots .  Reporting for Public Services Committee , Mr. Case  moved to approve on

 Development Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Leader Custom Homes, LLC, 
for regrading, installation and construction of sidewalks, utilities, drainage features and 
landscaping on City property in conjunction with development of the Willow Heights Subdivision. 
(Postponed from May 11, 2020) Mr. Luna moved to approve  in an amount not to exceed 
$49,074.00, seconded by Mr. White.  Mr. Luna moved to amend by striking #2 (A) and re-lettering 
and by removing the 2 nd, 3rd, 4th & 5th sentences in the 2nd paragraph of the 04-27-20 staff memo 
relating to compensation of property u se, seconded by Mr. White.  Charles Bloom, Planning and 
Development Director,  explained the  applicant requested the compensation requirement be 
withdrawn from the development agreement.  Casey Palma, Steil Surveying, agent for the 
applicant, advised the project will allow for infill development .  Support was expressed for  the 
requirement for compensation but not for this particular project.  It was noted  this is an unusual 
site with the development being adjacent to Greenway and that there may be occasions when use 
of City property should be paid for by developer .  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body  present.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present. (#7176)

 Consideration of  Bid # E-19-20 for five (5) new ten yard tandem dump trucks with 
spreaders and snow plows for the City of Cheyenne, Street & Alley Division (2015-2018 1% Sales 
Tax).  James Balzer, Peterbilt of Wyoming, provided detailed history and current information 
relating to past and current bid specifications for city equipment and  the bidding process.  He 
further expanded on qualifying and non -compliant competitors, bid processes regionally with 
respect to specifications, vendor preferences, and voiced support for the City’s bidding process.   
Patrick Crank, legal representative for Floyd’s Truck Center, voiced objection to awarding the bid 
to Peterbilt of Wyoming, noting he authored a letter on behalf of his client which was provided to 
members of the governing body.  Mr. Crank cited specific objections to various equipment 
specifications including required location of mirror mount placement of side mirrors, history of 
equipment bids awarded by the City, truck length, cab configuration, bid quotes  and p otential 
funding savings to the City and Floyd’s  Truck Center’s ability to satisfy bid  qualifications.  
Michael Gillam, Floyd ’s Truck Center, expressed objections to the City’s bidding process, 
specification qualifications, past bid award history and provided details regarding equipment 
differences between Peterbilt and Freightliner equipme nt.  Mr. Gillam advised he feels City 
equipment bids contain narrow specification writing making it difficult for vendors other than 
Peterbilt to be successful bidders for the City.  Randy Hickman, Street & Alley Manager, advised 
an equipment comparison was held on Wednesday, May 20th in which members of the governing 
body were invited to attend.  Mr. Hickman provided an explanation of bid requirements in terms 
of size requirement and noted a “dog house” means the engine is partially in the cab.  Mr. Hickman 
advised the “dog house” requirement provides more leg room  which is v ery important  and 
placement of mirror mounts can prevent shaking and vibration issues, noting safety is priority.  He 
  
further advised that w orking during snowstorm s is difficult with li mited visibility and the City 
can’t afford to compromise vision with mirror placement.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Hickman and Bryan 
Bell, Fleet Maintenance Manager, provided additional specification details and history of lifecycle 
for large equipment .  V icki Nemecek, Public Works Director, provided City bid history 
information and details relating to snow plows and sanitation trucks.  Ms. Nemecek reported there 
are 2 examples from last year of bids awarded to Floyd’s Truck Center w hich resulted in their 
inability to deliver equipment as represented and could not meet specifications.   She further 
elaborated on equipment requirement needs and specification compliance .  Mr. Luna moved to 
accept the bid from Peterbilt of Wyoming, Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $1,263,725.00, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal .  Members of the governing body provided comments relating to the 
following topics:  Specification writing currently being done in house and inquired about options 
for 3rd party specification writing, different opinions regarding recent equipment demonstration, 
legal obligations involving bid award process, concerns over equipment cost differences and minor 
equipment requirements.  M otion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governi ng body 
present with the exception of Mr. Case and Mr. Luna voting “no”.   
 
 Consideration of Bid # E-20-20 for three (3) new 2020 -2021 twin-screw diesel powered  
cab & chassis for the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation Division (Solid Waste Fund).  Michael Gillam, 
Floyd’s Truck Center, noted the same principals apply to this bid as the previous bid (plow trucks) 
and advised he feels the specifications were written narro wly.  James Balzer, Peterbilt of 
Wyoming, reported equipment by  Peterbilt and Kenworth are built separate in multiple ways, 
noting many vendors meet City specifications.   Upon inquiry, Vicki Nemec ek, Public Works 
Director, reported it is common for special equipment to be specified for sanitation equipment, 
noting issues exist with operations involving trash pick up and landfill dumping.  She further stated 
the Public Works Department is not opposed to having a 3 rd party bid specification writer, noting 
currently funding is unavailable for this service.  Discussion topics included:  Types of and 
placement of equipment, specification requirements, lifecycle of equipment and landfill equipment 
necessities.  Mr. Luna moved to accepted bid from Peterbilt of Wyoming, Cheyenne, in an amount 
not to exceed $441,700.00, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Case and Mr. Luna voting “no”.    
 
 Consideration of Bid # E-21-20 for 2020-2021 cab & chassis, single arm & dual arm 
automated loaders for the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation Division (Solid Waste Fund ).  Michael 
Gillam, Floyd’s Truck Center, and James Balzer, Peterbilt of Wyoming, spoke in sup port of the 
bid being awarded to Peterbilt of Wyoming.   Mr. Luna moved to accept the bid from Peterbilt of 
Wyoming, Cheyenne,  in an amount not to exceed $1,898,610.00 , seconded by Mr.  Roybal.  
Comments were made regarding specification writing process involving desire for a 3 rd party 
writer/review.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  present with 
the exception of Mr. Case and Mr. Luna voting “no”.   
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid # E-22-20 for 2020-2021 ½ ton, 4 door cr ew cab, full size pickup 
trucks, 4x4, 6 ½ foot box, with Fleet Work Package for the City of Cheyenne, Compliance Division 
and Sanitation Division (General Fund & Solid Waste Fund).   Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried ( accepted bid from Spradley Barr Motors, Cheyenne, in an amount not  to exceed 
$133,492.00). 
  
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-24-20 for furnishing Bituminous Asphalt Hot Mix, Tack Oil and 
Cold Mix on an annual basis for the City of Cheyenne, Street & Alley Division (2015-2018 1% 
Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried ( accepted bids from Knife River d/b/a JTL 
Group Inc., Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $29,000.00 (cold mix) and Simon Contractors, 
Cheyenne, in an amount not exceed $312,854.40 (hot mix and tack oil)).  
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-25-20 for furnishing Aggregates on an annual basis for the City 
of Cheyenne, Street & Alley Division (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried (accepted bid from Knife River d/b/a JTL Group Inc. , Cheyenne, in an  amount not to 
exceed $36,250.00). 
 
[CA] Transfer of retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Ace 
Majestic, Inc. d/b/a Redwood Lounge and Package Liquors, 2105 E. Lincolnway, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming (license currently held by Deep South LLC d/b/a Redwood Lounge).   Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Request to expand seating area for consumption relating to restaurant liquor license held 
by Central Café LLC d/b/a Central Café, 3920 Central Ave., Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented. 
 
Announcement was made  of Public Hearing to be held June 8, 2020 at 6:00 p.m. via 
electronic conference meeting, for a transfer of retail liquor license application filed with the City 
Clerk’s Office for Big D Oil Co., d/b/a Big D Oil #29, 100 North Greeley Hwy., Cheyenne, 
Wyoming. (license currently held by Appaloosa Enterprises LLC d/b/a Sanford’s Grub & Pub). 
 
Announcement was made  of Public Hea ring to be held June 8, 2020 at 6:00 p.m. via 
electronic conference meeting, for a bar and grill liquor license application filed with the City 
Clerk’s Office for Appaloosa Enterprises, LLC d/b/a Sanford’s Grub & Pub, 115 E. 17 th St., 
Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
 Under other business: 
 
  Mr. White inquired if there is a plan in place for when  the City Municipal Building will 
re-open and when the governing body and public may meet again in person.  Mike O’Donnell, 
City Attorney, advised the decision to re-open the Municipal Building is driven by the Governor’s 
statewide health orders.  He noted currently the City is practicing under P hase I which doesn’t 
allow for public meetings and advised it is anticipated that additional information will be released 
this week regarding status of re-opening under Phase II.  Upon further clarification, Mr. O’Donnell 
explained part of the difficulty currently is high risk individuals would be unable to attend public 
meetings, making meeting the requirements of the Public Meetings Act difficult to achieve.     
  
 There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:02 p.m.  
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 
 
 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  June 3, 2020

MEETING: City Council Meeting - Agenda Only | DATE: 2020-06-08 | CLIP_ID: 750 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 750

  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
June 8, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:07 p.m. via electronic conference meeting.  Attending electronic conference meeting  were:  
MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete 
Laybourn, Mike Luna,  Dr. Mark Rinne , Scott Roybal,  Dicky Shanor and Jeff White .  Also 
attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk.    
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.  Upon request by Mr.  Shanor, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Cook and Dr. Rinne, agenda item #19 was removed from the Consent Agenda. 
Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received by Dr. Rinne and Mr. Esquibel, 
agenda items #28(b) and #28(b)  were removed from the Consent Agenda.   Mr. Luna moved to 
approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body.   
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on May 26, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing for a transfer of retail liquor license application filed 
with the City Clerk’s Office for Big D Oil Co., d/b/a Big  D Oil #29, 100 North Greeley Hwy., 
Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held by Appaloosa Enterprises LLC d/b/a Sanford’s Grub 
& Pub).  There being no comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor 
Orr closed the hearing and referred the application to the Finance Committee.   
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing for a bar and grill liquor license application filed with 
the City Clerk’s Office for Appaloosa Enterprises, LLC d/b/a Sanford’s Grub & Pub, 115 E. 17 th 
St., Cheyenne, Wyoming.  There being no comments or input and purpose of the hearing having 
been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing and referred the application to the Finance Committee.  
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Approving and establishing revised water and sewer 
rates, tap and system development fees, administrative fees, service charges and penalties, and 
septic and commercial waste dump rates, as recommended by the Board of Public Utilities .  
Reporting for Finance Committee,  Mr. Luna moved to approve on third and final  reading, 
seconded by Mr . Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body .  
(#4308) 
 
  
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Sections 1.4.3, 2.1.1, 2.1.2, and 4.2.5 of the 
Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to define and clarify the purpose 
of remainder lots.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Case moved to approve on third   
and final reading, seconded by Mr.  Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body.  (#4309) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Section 2.2.6 of the Unified Development 
Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to modify time limits associated with temporary uses . 
Mr. Case moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4310) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Sections of Article 2 of the Unified 
Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to modify posted notice requirements for 
site plans and administrative approvals, clarify procedures for modification of site plans, modify 
authority to initiate text amendments, and modify criteria for variance approval.  Mr. Case moved 
to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#4311)  
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming for various purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing 
the amount of general and special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said 
appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2020 and ending June 30, 2021.  (Postponed 
from May 26, 2020 ).  Joe Dougherty provided comments, concerns and inquiries relating t o 
proposed budget allocations, use of reserves since September 2018, general fund expenditures, 
responsibility to tax payers, use of Solid Waste Fund and previous salary adjustments for specific 
staff members.  Mayor Orr advised responses to Mr. Dougherty’s inquires would be fourth coming 
and reported some pay increases were in response to inadequacie s involv ing salary and 
marketability. Mayor Orr expressed pride in her administration for bringing most employees up to 
market minimum. Comments were made regarding resolutions that outlined reappropriations that 
were directed towards courthouse project and a reappropriation to balance the budget. Amber Ash, 
DDA Executive Director, advised she was available for any questions and stated she appreciates 
DDA budget consi derations.  Lynn Huylar, Safe Harbor,  provided information and statistics 
related to child abuse and unemployment and explained that by removing funding for the agency, 
this may result in an increase of future cases.  Mr. Cook expressed appreciation to Ms. Huylar for 
her comments and the importance of funding social services programs such as Safe Harbor.  Upon 
inquiry by Mayor Orr, Ms. Huylar advised that CARES Act funding was not designated to non -
profits during the first round of CARES Act allocations. Ms. Huylar reported the agency is actively 
looking into other grant opportunities and funding sources, noting fund raising has been halted due 
to the COVID-19 Pandemic.  Mayor Orr provided additional information relating to current and 
projected CARES Act funding opportunities.  Heidi McNeil, Alcohol Receiving Center (ARC), 
provided comments relating to the importance and success of the program and commented on 
support she has received from the community and prior program participants . Ms. McNeil 
expressed concern for the future of the program and the lives of those affected by alcohol addiction 
and inquired as to what agencies/programs may be able to take up the work  if the center closes . 
Comments were expressed regarding appreciation of the program and see king a solution to the 
  
funding deficit.  Erin LeBlanc, Laramie County Senior Services, advised she has received positive 
reinforcement for the program from the community since the last council meeting and reinforced 
the need to continue  funding for the program.  Ms. LeBlanc expressed deep concern regarding 
what the budget cut would mean for meal service s for senior citizens and advised that due to the 
pandemic, they are serving additional meals.  Mayor Orr advised that the CARES Act is currently 
working toward working with seniors and food needs.  Ms. LeBlanc, upon inquiry, advised they 
have started receiving some CARES Act funds that are very Covid-19 specific.   Comments were 
made regarding uncertainty felt during the pandemic by seniors relating to the health and available 
services.  Mayor Orr advised that due to the uncertainty of future funding availability, she 
anticipates the FY 2021 budget will be revised as necessary moving forward which may result in 
funding becoming available for certain agencies.  Cara Carpenter and Charles Detridge commented 
on a peaceful protest they recently attended against police brutality and thanked Mayor Orr for her 
attendance.  They further inquired as to the Police Chief and City’s position on th e following 
issues:  Defunding of Police Departments, current and proposed budgets, use of force training, use 
of appropriate resources for mental health issues, crime and racial divide .  Chief Brian Kozak, 
Police Department,  advised there is an  annual rep ort on the city  website involving data of 
demographics of crimes and calls for service.  Chief Kozak advised his department is launching a 
new diversion program where people can be diverted to support programs for addiction and mental 
health issues instead of being arrested.  Upon inquiry, information was provided related to non -
profit vs. for profit status of jails, prisons and transitional services. Mayor Orr expressed 
appreciation to the Police Department and acknowledged the departments multiple recognitions.  
Chief Kozak reported the Police Department  follows best practices based on recommendations 
and commented on the following: citizens who are in crisis , extensive training, robust volunteer 
program and a citizens advisory committee that makes sure they are addressing community needs.  
He encouraged the public to attend the citizens academy and get involved with  the department.  
Upon inquiry, Chief Kozak provided information on 2 armored vehicles the department has and 
what they are used for based on policy.   Brandi Bristow and Karen Miller spoke in opposition of 
defunding of ARC program, provided personal history of their  success through the program and 
spoke in support of the pro gram and program funding and requested consideration for continued 
funding of the program.  Mayor Orr explained that the budget may be reviewed quarterly in a 
measured approach to determine what services could be funded or partially funded throughout the 
fiscal year and that some services are being funding through the CARES Act  and clarified the 
hospital is given funding to provide to the social service providers.    Karen Miller, ARC, further 
noted she appreciates the approach as the pandemic has been catastrophic and advised s ervices 
provided through ARC are more cost effective than when they are provided at the hospital .  
Reporting for Committee of the Whole, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded 
by Mr.  White.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by increasing the Fire Department’s budget by  
$57,687.00 and by raising the revenue from reserves in the same amount, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  
Mayor Orr advised that pursuant to Roberts Rules of Order, she is requesting all future 
amendments for the budget ordinance be submitted in writing prior to the final budget reading on 
06-16-2020 for the benefit of the public and to allow the City Treasurer time to calculate proposed 
changes.  Mr. Shanor provided information on the amendment explaining  the initial proposed 
budget had a shortage of a little over  $300,000 related to the collective bargaining agreement 
including 3 employees and longevity pay that was not included in the budget, after negotiations 
and including a reduction, the remaining unfunded liability  is what the amendment is for.   Dr. 
  
Rinne confirmed the latest figure provided by the City T reasurer is $57,687.00.  Michael 
O’Donnell, City Attorney, noted that the motion by Dr. Rinne is well taken based on discussion 
that occurred at the 5-26-2020 Special Meeting.  Robin Lockman , City Treasurer, confirmed the 
dollar amounts presented by Dr. Rinne are correct.   Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” all 
members of the governing body.  Mayor Orr upon inquiry, confirmed that budget amendments are 
required to be submitted in writing a few hours prior to the Special Meeting on 6-16-2020 to allow 
for publication for the benefit of the public and to allow the treasurer to perform accurate 
calculations.  Mayor Orr further stated she does not preside over the Committee of the Whole and 
that if amendments are to be submitted in writing prior to that meeting, Dr. Rinne as Council 
President will make that determination.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body.   The following is a summary of the proposed budget , including the 
aforementioned amendments : General Fund, $ 50,572,083; Weed and Pest, $ 539,308; Youth 
Alternatives Grants, $ 553,690; Development Impact Fees, $ 175,000; Recreation Programs, 
$1,548,033; Community Development Block Grant, $ 790,555; Law Enforcement Grants, 
$196,278; Transportation Planning, $ 888,242; Transit, $ 2,644,634; Juvenile Justice, $ 93,725; 
Special Friends, $ 90,189; One Percent Sales Tax, $11,033,691; Specific Purpose Optio n Tax,  
$673,535 Youth Activities, $ 18,000; Golf Facilities, $ 400,000; Fleet Maintenance, $ 3,969,148; 
Solid Waste Management  Fund, $26,564,476; Civic Center, $ 1,905,363; Ice & Events Center, 
$691,404; Perpetual Care, $ 5,000; Total City, $ 103,352,354; Water Enterprise, $ 34,310,945; 
Sewer Enterprise, $14,661,650; Total Appropriation, $152,324,949. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 5.12.110, Open Container-Sales and 
Consumption-Exceptions, and Section 5.12.120, Special Use Areas -Special Event Permit of 
Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 4, Business Licenses and 
Regulations, of the Municipal Code of the City o f Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Luna moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Mr. Roybal moved to amend by sub stitute 
dated 6-1-2020, seconded by Mr. White.  Mr. Roybal reviewed the substitute amendments which 
include creating special areas or districts in which possession of open containers and consumption 
of alcoholic beverages are permitted.  Amber Ash, DDA Executive Director, advised the proposal 
for open consumption is something that was recently approved in Casper and that a resolution must 
be adopted specifically for events, noting this requirement will allow for individual evaluation of 
proposed events.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Dr. Rinne commented on 
the process of the resolution affiliated with this ordinance relating to a proposed Summertime on 
Streets event scheduled for June 20th.  It was requested that this item be heard on third and final 
reading at the 6-16-2020 Special Meeting of the Governing Body.  City Clerk Kris Jones explained 
there was discussion of agenda items #12 and #26 being heard on third and final reading at the 6-
16-2020 Special Meeting of the Governing Body.  City Attorney Michael O’Donnell advised this 
item is referred to Finance Committee on 6 -15-2020 and will appear on  the agenda for special 
meeting next Tuesday night.   Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 8.40.063, Food Service 
Establishments-Sidewalk Cafes, of Chapter 8.40, Food and Food Service Establishments, of Title 
8, Health and Safety, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Luna moved 
to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Mr. White explained that this item and the 
  
previous agenda item are companion agenda items and will be heard on third and final reading at 
the 6 -16-2020 Special Meeting of the Governing Body.   Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Granting a Non -Exclusive Franchise to Union 
Telephone Company within the rights -of-way of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Luna 
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Section 2.76.020, Members -Terms-

 Contract Modification # 3 to Contract #6918 between the City of Cheyenne and FCI 
Constructors of Wyoming, LLC, to remove Furniture, Fixtures and Equipment (FF&E) Budget 
(Specific Purpose Sales Tax).  Ben Hornok, TC Solutions and Project Manager, explained that the 
furniture was originally placed in the FCI Constructors budget and the modification would move 
it to City to control and manage this portion of the project budget .  Mr. Hornok confirmed the 
modification amount of $107,500.00 would be removed from FCI’s portion of the project budget.    
Comments were made supporting FCI’s project management.   Mr. Hornok provided additional 
information relating to management of the project and efforts to keep the project on track for 
completion explaining there are potential delays resulting from the unexpected Covid -19 
Pandemic.  Mr. Hornok reported procurement of the furniture is in process.   Upon inquiry, Mr. 
Hornok advised that he believes this change will provide greater control to ensure the project stays 
on track concerning budget funding.   City Attorney  Michael O’Donnell  reported this project 
management approach has been historically successful.   Joe Dougherty voiced concerns relating 
management of the project funding.  Mr. Luna moved to approve in an amount not to exceed 
$107,500.00, seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Comments were made supporting the modification  and 
budget management of the project’s budget.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook voting “no”. (#6918) 
  
 
 Contract Modification #3 to Contract #6924 between the City of Cheyenne and TC 
Solutions, LLC, to add purchase and management of furniture, equipment, building 
commissioning, moving and signage.  (Specific Purpose Sales Tax) . Ben Hornok, TC Solutions,  
operating as owner representative for project, advised this modification will allow for the greatest 
city control of the project budget for quality and risks associated with the remainder of project.  He 
advised he has been w orking with staff to make sure  the project is completed as needed for 
functionality.  Mr. Luna moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $453,382.00, seconded by 
Mr. Esquibel.  Motion carried.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
with the exception of Mr. Cook voting “no”.  (#6924)

(#6927)

(2018-2022 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6976)

unforseen site conditions which required  design and construction modifications  (6th Penny Sales 
Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E-23-20 for One (1) New 2019-2020 Daihatsu Hi-Jet S510P Mini-
Truck for the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Botanic Gardens.  (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred 
to Finance Committee. 
 
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 Announcement was made of Special Meeting to be held Tuesday, June 16, 2020 at 6:00 
p.m. via electronic conference meeting, for proposed budgets for the City of Cheyenne and Board 
of Public Utilities for Fiscal Year 2020. 
 
 Announcement was made of Public Hearing to be held Tuesday, June 16, 2020 at 6:00 p.m. 
via electronic conference meeting, for proposed budgets for the City of Cheyenne and  Board of 
Public Utilities for Fiscal Year 2020. 
      
 Under other business: 
 
 Mr. Esquibel commended Chief Kozak and the Police Department for meeting with the  
community last week and inquired as to the status of the Friday’s on the Plaza event.  Mayor Orr 
advised it is anticipated that a further lift of restrictions of outdoor events by the Governor may be 
announced on June 15th, therefore allowing smaller scaled events including events on the plaza.   
  
 
 Mayor Orr reported that  summer seasonal and part time hiring will occur  for opening 
Community Recreation & Events facilities for services such as aquatics and ballfields. 
 
 Mayor Orr expressed appreciation to  Chief Kozak and the Police Department  for their 
integrity and training involving national recent events. 
 
 There being no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was
