MEETING: Special Meeting of the Governing Body - Agenda Only | DATE: 2020-06-16 | CLIP_ID: 752 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 752

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
 
Office of City Clerk 
 
June 16, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne convened in a special meeting on this date 
beginning at 6:00 p.m. via WebEx Electronic Conference Meeting .  Attending electronic 
conference meeting were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Mr. Case, Mr. 
Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor and Mr. White. 
Also attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  
 
[Clerk’s note:  Dr. Rinne was able to hear the proceedings but was unable to communicate due to 
audio difficulty. Dr. Rinne cast affirmative votes for all agenda items and amendments presented 
through alternative technology methods] 
  
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Section 5.12.110, Open Container-Sales and 
Consumption-Exceptions, and Section 5.12.120, Special Use Areas -Special Event Permit of 
Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 4, Business Licenses and 
Regulations, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Luna moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White.  M otion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#4312)        
 
 ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Section 8.40.063, Food Service 
Establishments-Sidewalk Cafes, of Chapter 8.40, Food and Food Service Establishments, of Title 
8, Health and Safety, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Luna moved 
to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#4313)  
     
 RESOLUTION – Creating a Special District for the ‘Summertime on the Streets Event’ to 
be held June 20, 2020, in which the possession of open containers is permitted between the hours 
of 11:00 a.m. and 4:00 p.m.  Alessandra McCoy Fakelman, Deputy City Attorney , provided 
information on the resolution and affiliated ordinance stating  the items a llow special areas or 
districts in which the possession of open containers is permitted and certain liquor licensees can 
participate. The periods of time and boundaries are establ ished by resolution adopted by the 
governing body. This particular resolution is for a special event to occur on Saturday, June 20 th 
from 11:00 a.m. to 4:00 p.m.  Ms. McCoy-Fakelman advised that upon feedback from the Police 
Chief, a proposed amendment contains specificity for designated certain types of cups, wristbands 
and doesn’t allow for any outside liquor.   Amber Ash, D owntown Development Authority  
Executive Director, provided event details and advised the event will be in compliance with the 
governor’s health orders .  She further confirmed that compliance will be followed and 
implemented in efforts to limit COVID-19 exposure.  Mr. Luna moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. 
Case.  Mr. Roybal moved to amend by substitute dated June 16, 2020, seconded by Mr. White.  
Mr. Roybal e xplained the substitute provides additional safeguards including easy to identify 
specific wristband and cup requirements and will not allow for outside liquor.  Upon inquiry, Ms. 
Ash provided details involving type, use and reuse of cups and wristbands .  Motion to a mend 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion  as amended carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#6065) 
 
 Mayor Orr opened a public hearing for proposed budgets for the City of Cheyenne and 
Board of Public Utilities for Fiscal Year 2021. There being no comments or input and purpose of 
the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing.        
 
   ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming for various purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing 
the amount of general and special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said 
appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2020 and ending June 30, 2021.  Mr. White 
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. White moved to amend 
by adding footnotes #1-13 and adding language that the City Treasurer has the authority as part of 
this amendment to make any technical amendments to these footnotes and the budget ordinance to 
reflect the intent of these footnotes , seconded by Mr. Roybal .  Mayor Orr clarified the  footnotes 
are from the June 10, 2020 Committee of the Whole meeting.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body.  Mr. White moved to amend Footnote #2 to include: 
This footnote also excludes seasonal workers and does not prohibit an employee form being 
promoted into a pre -existing higher position with a pre -existing higher salary, seconded by Mr. 
Luna.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. Upon inquiry, 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer, confirmed there were no additional amendments needed as a 
result of recent discussions with the Fire Union relating to the Fire Department ’s budget.  Main 
motion as twice amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  (#4314) 
 
 Mr. Luna expressed appreciation to City Treasurer Robin Lockman, City Council and City 
staff members for their dedicated work on the budget.  Mayor Orr echoed Mr. Luna’s comments 
and advised that due to the unprecedented times, the budget may need to be reviewed through out 
the fiscal year.                
 
 
      Submitted by, 
 
 
       Kristina F. Jones 
       City Clerk 
 
 This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  June 24, 2020

June 22, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning

MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete 
Laybourn, Mike Luna,  Dr. Mark Rinne , Scott Roybal,  Dicky Shanor and Jeff White .  Also

 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to

in its normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Case and Mr. Shanor, agenda item #22 (a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon 
request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook and Mr. Roybal, agenda item 
#22 (b) was removed from the Consent  Agenda.  Mr. Luna moved to approve remaining items, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.

Company within the rights -of-way of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.   Reporting for Finance 
Committee, Mr. Luna moved to approve on third and final  reading, seconded by Mr.  White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4315) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 2.76.020, Members -Terms-
Vacancies, of Chapter 2.76, Downtown Development Authority, of Title 2, Administration and 
Personnel, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming . Amber Ash, Exec utive 
Director of the Downtown Development Authority, advised she was available for questions.  Upon 
inquiry, Ms. Ash explained that the DDA B oard received applications from three qualified 
members, and with only two available seats open on the Board, they are requesting to expand the 
number of members from 9 to 10.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Case moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Comment was made on the need to expand  
the membership  based on submitted qualified  applications and the history of  board member 
uncompleted terms.  Ms. Ash reported that per State Statute, the board can have a total of 11 
members.   Mayor Orr cautioned having an even numbered membership.  Ms. Ash noted that items 
  
considered for approval would require 6 votes to pass.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.

provide additional funds for unforeseen preparatory work to the underlying road surface.  (2015 -

Truck for the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Botanic Gardens (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion

[CA] Consideration of Bid # S-14-20 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Simon

Transfer of retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Big D Oil 
Co., d/b/a Big D Oil #29, 100 North Greeley Hwy., Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held 
by Appaloosa Enterprises LLC d/b/a Sanford’s Grub & Pub) .  Mr. Luna moved to approve, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Comment was ma de related to retail liquor licenses  being utilized by 
convenience stores and how it is the least effective use of that type of license. As there are currently 
no available retail liquor licenses, they are an asset to the city and should be utilized in the best 
way possible to promote economic development.  Motion carried.   Voting “yes” – Mr. Esquibel, 
Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna, Mayor Orr , Mr. Shanor and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. 
Cook, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Roybal. 
 
 Bar and grill liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Appaloosa 
Enterprises, LLC d/b/a Sanford’s Grub & Pub, 115 E. 17th St., Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Clyde Mead, 
Big D Oil, speaking on the previous agenda item (transfer of retail liquor license applicatio n) 
provided information relating to staff training relating to meeting state and local liquor licensing 
regulations and reported customers have requested the service at the south Big D Oil location.  Mr. 
Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.   Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body.          
 
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented. 
 
Reappointments of Jordan Evans, Terry Roker, Jennifer Lundberg and Barbara Fecht to 
the Public Transit Advisory Board; Lieutenant Eric Kent, Lieutenant David Janes, Kathy Emmons, 
Kimberly Skoutary -Johnson and Carla Thurin to the Laramie County Juvenile Ser vice Joint 
  
Powers Board; Dr. Jasper James Chen to the Cheyenne/Laramie County Economic Development 
Joint Powers Board; Barry Sims, Tyler McLachlan and Tony O’Brien to the Tourism Promotion 
Joint Powers Board.  Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Royb al.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” - all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not 
vote (mayoral appointments). 
 
Appointments of Kristina Richardson and Molly Palmer to the Mayor’s Council for People 
with Disabilities.  Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” - all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote 
(mayoral appointments). 
 
 Under other business: 
 
 Mr. White referenced a recent letter to the editor in the Wyoming Tribune Eagle that was 
complementary of  work performed by the City Forestry crews and arborists that were sent to 
Laramie to help with tree issues after a damaging snow storm.  Mr. White expressed appreciation 
to the DDA, T hankful Thursdays and Lyn n Buys Houses for  the recent ‘ Summertime on the 
Streets’ event, noting he is unaware of any open container issues associated with the event and that 
downtown businesses were appreciative for additional business.  Mayor Orr echoed Mr. White’s 
comments regarding appreciation for the City’s Forestry crew.  
 
 Mr. Esquibel advised he had been contacted by citizens who expressed concern relating to 
a recent Police and Community Together meeting who feel forums of this nature should not be 
held at Police Headquarter s and instead in a neutral location .  Mr. Esquibel advised a forum has 
been scheduled at the Laramie County Library on July 18th.  Up inquiry, Mr. Esquibel advised no 
law enforcement members will be present, elected officials will attend by invitation only, it will 
be open to the public and  all panelists have been predetermined.  Mayor Orr reported the local 
Chapter of NAACP will meet on Saturday, June 27th from 10:00 a.m. to Noon at Element Church 
to allow for marginalized citizens voices to be heard.  
 
 There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:43 p.m.  
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEETING: Special Meeting of the Governing Body - Agenda Only | DATE: 2020-06-29 | CLIP_ID: 756 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 756

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
June 29, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met a special meeting on this date beginning 
at 6:27 p.m. via electronic conference meeting.  Attending electronic conference meeting  were:  
MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; C OUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Pete Laybourn, 
Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Absent -Ken Esquibel.  
Also attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk.    
 
RESOLUTION – Providing for the City of Cheyenne to apply for “CARES ACT” and 
other federally available funds to address the COVID -19 Pandemic.  (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) Mr. 
Shanor abstained from voting due to  personal employment affiliation with the Wyoming State 
Loan and Investment Board.  Mr. Roybal moved to suspend the rules pursuant to State Statute §15-
1-115 to consider the resolution for final adoption, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.   Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Shanor  who didn’t 
vote.  Mr. Case moved to amend by adding the following language: “All applications to any entity 
shall be aligned to the CARES Act spending priorities passed as part of the City’s FY2021 budget 
ordinance”, seconded by Mr. Luna. Michael O’Donnell, City Attorney, expressed concern and 
urged caution in approving the proposed amendment relating to annual budget requirements and 
proposed grant funding regulations.  Mr. Case with concurrence from Mr. Luna, withdrew the 
proposed amendment.  Mr. White moved to amend by adding the following language in the Now 
Therefore Be It Resolved clause following the wor d pandemic “in an amount not to exceed five 
million dollars”, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Shanor who didn’t vote. Main motion as 
amended carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of 
Mr. Shanor who didn’t vote. (#6070) 
  
RESOLUTION – Encouraging summer events at the Cheyenne Depot Plaza.   (Sponsors – 
Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor & Mr. White) – 
referred to Public Services Committee. 
       
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held July 13, 2020 at 6:00 p.m. via  
electronic conference meeting, to determine whether compliance of annexation conditions required 
by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for Lots 1 and 2 and a Portion of a 20 Foo t Vacated Alley 
being adjacent to and West of said Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, Prairie Hills Addition, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located northwest of the intersection of Yellowstone Road and Western Hills Blvd).  
  
 Mayor Orr stated the purpose of the special mee ting had been met, and there  being no 
further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially adjourned at  6:37 
p.m.  
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 
 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  July 10, 2020

OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
July 13, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:07 p.m. via electronic conference meeting.  Attending electronic conference meeting  were:  
MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete 
Laybourn, Mike Luna,  Dr. Mark Rinne , Scott Roybal,  Dicky Shanor and Jeff White .  Also 
attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk.    
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.   Mr. White moved to approve, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on June 22, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on  June 29,2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mayor Orr opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for Lots 1 and 2 and a Portion of  a 20 
Foot Vacated Alley being adjacent to and west of said Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, Prairie Hills Addition, 
Laramie County, Wyoming (located northwest of the intersection of Yellowstone Road and 
Western Hills Blvd).  There being no comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been 
met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Section 2.76.020, Members -Terms-
Vacancies, of Chapter 2.76, Downtown Development Authority, of Title 2, Administration and 
Personnel, of the Municipal  Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  Amber Ash, Downtown 
Development Authority Executive Director, advised she was available for any questions.  Upon 
inquiry, Ms. Ash and Mayor Orr provided information on the application process for 
boards/commissions explaining there are often more applications received than positions 
available.  Applications are kept until there is a vacancy and the applicant is contacted to see if 
they are still interested.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Case moved to approve on 
third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.   Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body.  (#4316) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the official zoning map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from LR (Low-Density Residential) to MUR (Mixed-
  
Use Residential Emphasis) for property generally located northeast of the intersection of Kelley 
Drive and Old Faithful Road.  Kari Happold, Rustic Wyoming Reality, advised she was available 
for any questions  and explained Rustic Wyoming Reality  is looking to expand.  Mr. Luna 
welcomed Ms. Happold to Ward III.  Tom Jacobson, #1 Properties, owner representative, voiced 
support for the ordinance.  Mr. Case moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. 
White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4317) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the official zoning map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from LI (Light Industrial) to MUB (Mixed -Use 
Business Emphasis) for property generally located northeast of the intersection of west 21st Street 
and Reed Avenue.  Matt Ashby, agent representing owner, advised he was available for any 
questions.   Mr. Case moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4318) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the official zoning map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from CB Community Business to MUB Mixed -Use 
Business Emphasis for a portion of the SE1/4 of Section 33, T.14N., R66W, 6 th P.M., Cheyenne, 
Wyoming (located south of and adjacent to Sparks Road, west of Ridge Road) . Jim Grenfell, 
Executive Director of Wyoming Housing Network, provided information on the  land and  
explained the anticipated use includes senior living units with garages and adequate parking.  Mr. 
Grenfell advised he has had discussions with Environmental Health regarding the potential to place 
a clinic on the property.  He further advised it is anticipated that the remaining 6 acres will be used 
for future multi-family low income housing units.  Mr. Case moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter 5.04, Business Licenses, Generally, 
and Chapter 5.08, Business License fees, and creating a new Chapter 5.77, Nicotine Product Sales, 
of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations; amending Chapter 8.64, Smoking in Public Places, 
of Title 8, Health and Safety; and amending Chapter 9.16, Offenses by or Against Minors, of Title 
9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. Brittany 
Wardle expressed appreciation to the governing body for consideration of the ordinance and 
advised that the proposed licensing allows restrictive measures that can be utilized  to help 
eliminate underage nicotine sales.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Luna moved to approve 
on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Mr. White advised the ordinance reflects State Statute 
modifications raising the age from 18 to 21 for purchasing tobacco/nicotine products.  Mr. White 
advised the Wyoming State Legislature did the same and noted that he, Ms. Wardle, Joe Chenchar, 
Assistant City Attorney , and a representative of the American Cancer Society reviewed the 
proposed ordinance changes.  Upon inquiry, Mr. White advised it is his understanding there were 
no comments received from small business owners or the Police Departme nt regarding the 
proposed changes.  Joe Chenchar, Assistant City Attorney, upon inquiry, advised he will seek 
verification of businesses being notified regarding the proposed changes.  Request for clarification 
of the proposed changes vs. existing law was made and noted it appears  there is a new license 
requirement and an increased fee, banning of vending machines and new eligibility requirements 
for licensees.  Mr. Chenchar advised the existing annual tobacco license fee is $65.00, noting the 
proposed increased fee was determined to assist with compliance check expenses.  Upon inquiry, 
  
Mr. Chenchar provided information relating to the appeal process involving annual violation s, 
noting it is the decision of the governing body to pursue suspension/revocation of a license.  Mr. 
Chenchar confirmed convictions would be through the Municipal Court.  Support was expressed 
for the ordinance and comment was made related to timing of raising license fees considering the 
current COVID-19 hardships on small businesses .  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Esquibel, 
Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Mayor 
Orr, Mr. Roybal, and Mr. Shanor. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter 10.52, Stopping, Standing and 
Parking Generally, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming. Mr. Case moved to withdraw the ordinance.  Dr. Rinne raised a point of 
order that the item could not be withdrawn stating that a fter an agenda item has been introduced, 
it belongs to the  governing body and can’t be withdrawn.  Mayor Orr asked if there were any 
objections to the motion to withdraw, to which Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne expressed objection.    
Michael O’Donnell, City Attorney, reported that after a quick review Roberts Rules of Order and 
concurrence from Alessandra McCoy-Fakelman, Deputy City Attorney, it would be appropriate to 
vote the motion to withdraw down.  Motion to withdraw failed.    Voting “no” – all members of 
the governing body.  [Clerk’s note: the item died due to the lack of a motion to approve]

RESOLUTION – Creating a Special District for ‘Cheyenne Days – Legendary Nights 
Events’, July 17, 2020 to July 26, 2020, in which the possession of open containers is permitted 
within the Downtown District Boundaries.   Amber Ash, Executive Director of  the Downtown 
Development Authority , explained ther e were initially several events planned but due to the 
COVID-19 pandemic, sponsors of the events made the decision to cancel them.  Ms. Ash reported 
  
the DDA evaluated the costs associated with the open container event and it was determined those 
costs were n’t justified without major events taking place.  Ms. Ash further advised  the Paint 
Slingers asked to remove their event from the event list along with concerts that were planned on 
that Wednesday and Friday.  Ms. Ash requested the item be withdrawn if possible or otherwise 
disposed of noting however, that a negative vote is not desired as future events could be considered.  
Comments included:  concurrence of the event  no longer being feasible, consumption area 
including entire DDA districts, special district resolution being a “pilot program”, support of the 
resolution in hopes it may help businesses in the area , management a nd enforcement for these 
types of activities and suggestions for more defined boundaries .  Ms. Ash advised she hasn’t 
specifically consulted with other communities about their open container events and that the DDA 
is looking to re -evaluate the district in  the future to use the Main Street District to define 
consumption boundaries as it is smaller and more contained.  Mr. Luna moved to adopt, seconded 
by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. Luna  moved to amend by substitute  dated July 8, 2020  seconded by Mr.  
Roybal.  Mr. White explained the amendment includes removing the Paint Slingers event from the 
event listing and increasing the event cup size from 12 ounces to 16 ounces.  Chief Brian Kozak, 
Police Department, explained it was his understanding that the intent of the open container 
approval was for a specific downtown area that could be effectively monitored, and each eligible 
establishment would be provided with a certain number of event wristbands and cups.  Ms. Ash 
advised the events that are  currently scheduled for Cheyenne Day are the Melodrama, Dueling 
Pianos and a potential block party and further advised the DDA will be promoting merchant sales 
and sidewalk sales.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
with exception of  Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  Chief Kozak clarified that there would be no 
additional police officer expense for Cheyenne Day as it is covered under their annual budget and 
noted open consumption without specific planned events may be less than ideal. Additional 
comments were made supporting the intent of the resolution, concerns with issues raised regarding 
costs and enforcement, lack of participation and current public  health environment.  Motion as 
amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. 
Laybourn, Mayor Orr, and Dr. Rinne  voting “no”.  [Clerk’s note: Dr. Rinne confirmed he was a 
“no” vote which wasn’t acknowledged du e to a delayed audio response involving electronic 
meeting lag time]  (#6071)

governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (mayoral appointments).     
  
 Under other business: 
 
 Margaret Thompson submitted a petition with signatures of residents living within in her 
building opposing the Saturday Cruise Nights relating to excessive noise during the events. Ms. 
Thompson shared her experiences with excessive noise and advised she has a noise meter that she 
has used to record excessively high readings.  She further commented on and provided information 
  
on noise limits as sited in current city code and requested consideration for an alternative location 
for the event.  Mayor Orr expressed appreciation t o Ms. Thompson for bringing forward a 
recommendation for an alternate location.  Chief Kozak advised the Police Department does have 
a sound meter and reported enforcement in this case is difficult due to the nature of this particular 
event as there are multiple vehicles involved and there isn’t a particular event organizer or entity 
sponsoring the event.  Chief Kozak further reported violations of this particular city code section 
are difficult to enforc e.  Mr. Laybourn expressed concerns relating to Ms. Thompson ’s and 
accompanying petitioner’s complaint and supported efforts to reach a resolution.     
 
 There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:16 p.m.  
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
             
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
July 27, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:07 p.m. via electronic conference meeting.  Attending electronic conference meeting  were:  
MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete 
Laybourn, Mike Luna,  Dr. Mark Rinne , Scott Roybal,  Dicky Shanor and Jeff White .  Also 
attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk.    
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.   Upon request by Mr.  White, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Cook and Mr. Luna, agenda item #23 (a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook and Mr. Case, agenda 
item #16 was removed from the Consent Agenda .  Mr. Luna moved to approve remaining items, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.

Consent Agenda) carried.

(#4319)

ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter 2.29, Community Service Officers,

carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main m otion as amended carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Defining the terms and conditions of employment for 
non-uniformed employees of the City for Cheyenne for Fiscal Year 2020 -2021.  Reporting for 
Finance Commit tee, Mr. Luna moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr.  White. 
Clarification was requested on vacation benefits regarding buyback program and amount allowed 
for annual carry over for both uniformed and nonuniformed employees.  Upon inquiry, Darrin 
Hass, Human Resources Director, clarified that nonuniformed employees can’t carry over anymore 
vacation hours annually than what is currently in the employee handbook  and provided t o 
uniformed employees and noted the only change for nonuniformed employees was the removal of 
longevity pay.  Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, explained  the reason the uniformed 
employees can carry over additional vacation hours is because they are the  only department that 
could sell time back  and that due to budget cuts , they were not allowed to sell time back.  He 
further noted that due to the inability to sell back hours, he wanted to allow them to keep more 
time on the books.  Mayor Orr and Robin Lockman, City Treasurer, confirmed nonuniformed 
employees do not get pay out for vacation leave  and have never been eligible for a buyback 
program.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Defining the terms and conditions of employment for 
sworn officers of the Cheyenne Police Department for Fiscal Year 2020 -2021.  Mr. Luna moved 
to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr.  Esquibel.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body.

RESOLUTION – Declaring as surplus a City -owned property generally located at the 
northeast corner of Morrie Avenue and East Lincolnway, together with improvements thereon, 
and authorizing the City to proceed with the process to dispose of the property pursuant to Wyo.  
Stat. §15-1-112(A).  Mr. Luna moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. White.  Upon inquiry, Charles 
Bloom, Planning & Development Director, reported he has had extensive discussion s with 
Community Recreation & Events  regarding what it would take for the parking lot to be brought 
into be part of the park .  He further advised it would require various updates including ADA 
improvements and noted Community Recreation & Events is not interested in retaining the 
property.  Mr. Bloom advised upon inquiry , there hasn’t been a market analysis done , however, 
based on the assessor’s analysis the property has an estimated value of $9.40/sq. ft. and that bidders 
would be required to provide an estimated value on the property based on an appraisal.  Mayor Orr 
reported upon inquiry that funding from the proposed sale of the property would go into Land 
Trust Fund established by the governing body.  Comments were made supporting the City 
obtaining an appraisal for the property.  Mr. Bloom confirmed that the purchase agreement would 
ultimately be approved by  the governing body and that could include that the City will get an 
appraisal.  Mayor  Orr advised the cost of the appraisal could be recouped from the Land Trust 
Fund.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#6076) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Approving the budget of the Cheyenne Downtown Development 
Authority for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2020 and ending June 30, 2021 .  Amber Ash, 
Downtown Development Authority Executive Director (DDA), advised the DDA Board has 
approved the budget.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Ash provided budget information including grant funding 
amounts and remaining reserves with a reserve balance of approximately $750,000.00, noting they 
are still finalizing final expenses from last year.   She advised the budget document sent to the 
governing body shows a proposed budget for the year and doesn’t include reserve accounts and 
what the DDA is drawing down for the year.  Comment was made that the budget document should 
include details of the reserves account.  Ms. Ash explained upon inquiry the DDA is looking at 
leveraging the reserve account for investments for developing projects. She further explained they 
are looking at the potential to purchase a property to flip or develop properties for lease and 
leveraging other funding sources for both of the possibilities.  Comments were made that due to 
funding challenges involving the COVID -19 Pandemic, the DDA is looki ng to find solutions to 
sustain some activities on their own.  Additional comments were made relating to City Code 
chapter 2.76, Duties of the Board, noting the proposed approach is very different than what is in 
City Code and noted the DDA Board shall pla n and propose plans of development.  Support was 
voiced for continuing the Façade Improvement Program as it had been a success.  Comments were 
made on the functionality of the DDA.  Concern was expressed related to the potential future DDA 
projects and the status of the Main Street program. Ms. Ash, upon inquiry provided an explanation 
of the proposed budget columns which includes CARES Act funding and reported that one of the 
biggest changes in the budget is for the downtown planters.  She explained that annual 
  
plants/shrubs were added so that only supplemental additions would necessary  each year.  She 
further explained the DDA worked with CFD to remove some of the planters and they will be 
looking at possibly updating planters with reservoirs in the long term. Ms. Ash also noted the DDA 
is looking to increase their private partnering ef forts for downtown events in the future to assist 
with funding needs.  Mr. Luna moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. (#6077)

 Under other business: 
  
 Mr. White expressed appreciation to the Mayor, Council Members and City staff that 
attended the Plaza Splash Pad ribbon cutting ceremony and thanked his colleagues for having the 
vision to fulfill the commitment and for seeing the project through.  He expressed additional 
appreciation to members of the public and private businesses who contributed to and partnered

 Mr. White noted the passing of Father Beavers from St. Mary’s church.   
 
 Mayor Orr read into the record a letter she authored relating to recently passed Resolutions 
#6071-Creating a Special District for ‘Cheyenne Days – Legendary Nights Events’, July 17, 2020 
to July 26, 2020, in which the possession of open containers is permitted within the Downtown 
District Boundaries and #6072-Encouraging Summer Events at the Cheyenne D epot Plaza.  She 
advised both resolutions went into effect without her signature.  Mayor Orr stated the following:  
Resolution #6071- Creating a Special District for ‘Cheyenne Days – Legendary Nights Events’, 
July 17, 2020 to July 26, 2020, in which the possession of open containers is permitted within the 
  
Downtown District Boundaries.  The resolution was originally requested by the Downtown 
Development Authority for our consideration.   Upon further review and concern of budgetary 
constraints for carrying out their request, the DDA provided public testimony that they no longer 
wished to pursue the allowances granted to them in this resolution and asked for a “no” vote.   I 
sided with the request of the DDA and was a “no” vote. This resolution passed with myse lf and 
Mr. Laybo urn as “no” votes and went into effect without my signature.   Resolution #6072 
- Encouraging Summer Events at the Cheyenne Depot Plaza.  Several months ago, the City of 
Cheyenne Community Recreation and Events Department submitted a proposa l to City County 
Health for consideration of hosting the Friday Nights at the Plaza concert series, creating gated 
areas restricting the number of each area to meet the state guidelines of 250 people in each 
area.  This plan was submitted when Cheyenne had  experienced no new cases, and we were 
hopeful this downturn would continue.   Unfortunately, that was not the case. Cheyenne 
experienced an uptick in cases. Subsequently, City department officials who submitted the plan to 
county health made it known to me that they no longer thought we could carry the plan out.  I want 
to remind you that these events have been so successful that we have seen as many as 7,000 people 
in attendance at our outdoor concerts.   And certainly not within the new social distancing 
guidelines.  I apologize I did not consult with you first, however a decision needed to be made 
quickly at the request of sponsors and contract acts.   As mayor, I believe the decision was well 
within my scope as mayor to protect the health and safety of our community knowing the fact that 
we could no longer carry out the plan as previously approved by City County Health. To do so 
would be wrong. This resolution passed with myself and Mr. Laybo urn as “no” votes and went 
into effect without my signature. 
 
 There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:11 p.m.  
 
 
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 
  
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  August , 2020 

---

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
August 10, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:07 p.m. via electronic conference meeting .  Attending electronic conference meeting  were:  
MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete 
Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White. Absent: Mike Luna.  Also 
attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk. 
 
 Expression of Appreciation to the City of Cheyenne by Wyoming Main Street. Matt Ashby 
representing Wyoming Main Street,  Chairman of the state Advisory Board, advised they are 
recognizing that the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority has been designated as a 2020 
accredited Main Street Program and the status is a top tier recognition. He provided information 
on the Main Street program including new number of businesses and employees in the state and 
city, volunteer hours, money raised for small businesses and communities involved in the program. 
He explained one reason they are recognizing the DDA is for their Covid-19 re sponse which 
included over $50,000 in relief grants provided to help keep businesses open. Mr. Ashby presented 
a certificate of appreciation to the City for their support of the nationally recognized Main Street 
approach and a certificate to DDA in recognition of their 2020 Main Street America  accredited 
program.  Mayor Orr expressed appreciation to the DDA and DDA Director Amber Ash.

in its normal sequence on the agenda. Mr. Shanor recused himself from discussion and voting due 
to business affiliation with agenda i tem #1 4.  Mr. White moved to approve, seconded by Mr. 
Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.

by Mr. Roybal.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing b ody 
present. 
 
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
  
 Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held August 24, 2020, at 6:00 p.m. via 
electronic conference meeting, for a transfer of ownership for a retail liquor license application 
filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Old Chicago Taproom II LLC d/b/a Old Chicago #74, 1734 
Meadowland Drive, Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held by Wadsworth Old Chicago Inc. 
d/b/a Old Chicago Restaurant).     
 
 Under other business: 
  
 Mr. Laybourn expressed appreciation for extra effort at a recent Cheyenne  Symphony 
Orchestra performance at the Civic Center by City staff and partners of the Cheyenne Symphony 
Orchestra. 
 
 Mr. Laybourn followed up on Downtown Development Authority budgetary concerns that 
were discussed during the last  city council meeting.  He expressed c oncern with cancelling the 
  
façade improvement program and the DDA’s intent to leverage the reserve account for investments 
and developing projects. Mr. Laybourn requested the City C ouncil president to schedule a work 
session and also for the DDA to hold a public meeting to inform citizens about their visions and 
intent for the program. 
  
 Mayor Orr advised a ribbon cutting ceremony for the new court house building will be 
scheduled in the next few weeks. She provided information on the first court date to be held in the 
new building and expressed appreciation to staff working on the project. 
 
 Mayor Orr congratulated Cheyenne Legion Post 6 baseball for winning state title and 
expressed appreciation to Community Recreation & Events staff for opening and maintaining the 
baseball fields.

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
August 24, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:07 p.m. via electronic conference meeting .  Attending electronic conference meeting  were: 
COUNCIL PRESIDENT – Dr. Mark Rinne; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, 
Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White . Absent: 
Mayor Marian J. Orr.  Also attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.  Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by  Mr. Roybal.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on August 10, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 President Rinne opened a public hearing for a transfer of ownership for a retail liquor 
license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Old Chicago Taproom I I LLC d/b/a Old 
Chicago #74, 1734 Meadowland Drive, Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently  held by 
Wadsworth Old Chicago Inc. d/b/a Old Chicago Restaurant). Brian Proffitt, attorney representing 
applicant, provided background on the transfer of ownership request advising the former license 
operator filed for bankruptcy earlier this year.  He explained the applicant purchased assets out of 
the bankruptcy and the establishment will continue to operate as an Old Chicago.  Julia Weaver, 
restaurant manager, advised she was available for questions.  It was noted for the record that Mr. 
Shanor joined the meeting in progress during public comment at 6:05 p.m.  There being no further 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, President Rinne closed the hearing 
and referred the item to the September 8, 2020 Finance Committee meeting.  
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 15.50, Historic 
Design Guidelines, of Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Luna) 
  
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Back 40 
Subdivision, 2nd Filing, a replat Lot 1, Block 1, Back 40 Subdivision, Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located north of West Lincolnway at Grant Avenue) .  Reporting for Public Services 
Committee, Mr. Ca se moved to adopt, seconded by Mr . Cook.  Mr. Case moved to amend by 
revised final plat map dated July 1, 2020, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#6081) 
  
RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of the Nita M. Lowey 21 st Century Learning 
Centers grant application to the Wyoming Department of Education on behalf of the Governing 
Body of t he City of Cheyenne to submit continuation application for year three of funding for 
afterschool programing and necessary support items.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Luna 
advised the item was reviewed  under other items at the August  17, 2020 Finan ce Committee  
meeting, therefore provisions of Title 2 of City Code have been met.  Mr. Luna moved to adopt, 
seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mr. Shanor abstained from discussion and voting due to work affiliation 
with the Wyoming Department of Education.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. (#6082) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of the Senior Corps 2021 RSVP Competition 
grant application to the Corporation for National and Community Service (CNCS) on behalf of the 
Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne to submit application for funding for Youth Alternatives, 
Special Friends RSVP Mentoring Program and necessary support items.   Mr. Luna advised the 
item was reviewed under other items at the August 17, 2020 Finance Committee meeting, therefore 
provisions of Title 2 of City Code have been met.  Mr. Luna moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. 
White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#6083) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Amending Resolution #3597, a Citizen Participation Plan, which 
establishes objectives, procedures, requirements, and responsibilities for the involvement of lower 
income individuals, as well as the community at large in the development of a City Comprehensive 
Five Year Housing and Community Development Plan – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor 
– Mr. Luna) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Accepting a grant in the amount of $150,000.00 from the Cheyenne 
Community Recreation District (Recreation Mill) for the replacement of the restroom/concession 
building at the Cheyenne Junior League Complex – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – 
Dr. Rinne) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Creating a Special District for the ‘Golf, it’s the Least of our Problems’ 
Golf Tournament to be held on September 26, 2020, in which the possession of open containers is 
permitted within the Downtown District Boundaries – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Approving water and sanitary sewer services, by outside user water and 
sanitary sewer services agreements, for SJT, LLC, for Tracts 21, 22, and 23, Green Subdivision, 
3rd Filing, situated in the SW¼SW¼ of Section 22, Township 14 North, Range 66 West, of the 6th 
P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Luna) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Supporting the State of Wyoming Governor’s proposal to have the State 
Loan and Investment Board consider modifying its rules to adopt an allocation model fo r 
distribution of Coronavirus Aid, Relief, and Economic Security Act (CARES ACT) funding to 
local governments.   City Attorney Michael O’Donnell provided information  on the resolution 
explaining G overnor Gordon proposed a method to distribute  CARES Act funding to local 
municipalities and counties utilizing a formula which considers population and number of Covid-
  
19 cases.  He explained $9 million would be allocated for Cheyenne and that Governor Gordon 
asked for comments on the split between municipalities and counties which the resolution includes.  
He noted a 50/50 split does not reflect where most citizens in the state reside as 69% percent reside 
in recognized municipalities.  The proposed resolution recommends the split distribution of 1/3 to 
2/3 (2/3 to municipalities) which accounts for where most residents reside.  It also asks for 
consideration of a  new process for distribution  of funds .  Mr. O’Donnell provided further 
information on the distribution process noting the approach of first come first serve wasn’t fair due 
to the application process and the resolution request is for a downstream distribution to cities and 
counties.  Mr. O’Donnell explained the City is willing to sign a hold harmless agreement, whereas 
if monies aren’t spent correctly, the city would be responsible to repay those monies.  T hese 
specific funds would be deposited into a special revenue fund account (essentially a trust fund and 
withdrawals can only take place in certain situations).  Funding requests would be presented to the 
Chief Executive Designee of the funds who would determine if the request is appropriate, noting 
funds must be spent by December 31, 2020.  It was noted the current process is cumbersome, slow, 
difficult and the proposed process would be efficient and would allow money distribution to be in 
a more rapid manner.  The City would be required to keep appropriate records which are subject 
to audit and would also be monitored by the City Treasurer.  Mr. O’Donnell advised the suggested 
path is for the City to adopt a statement of support that is included in the resolution. Upon inquiry, 
Mr. O’Donnell explained he has been working on the CARES Act funding application process for 
the City and due to the complexity of the current process and he drafted the resolution to provide 
efficiency and allow for rapid distribution of funds .  He further advised  the proposed idea was 
generally discussed with other communities and they seemed supportive and noted if adopted, he 
would reach out to through WAM to other communities.  Upon further inquiry, Mr. O’Donnell 
provided information involving the current process authorizing the City to submit grant 
applications in amounts not exceeding $5 million dollars, which was adopted by resolution in June 
of 2020.   He noted if a grant application exceeded that threshold, it would need to come before 
the governing body for approval.  It was confirmed that unless the resolution was postponed, the 
resolution is on final consideration .  Comments were provided related to  the limited timing 
involving the opportunity to apply for funding and support was voiced for  the resolution under 
suspension of the rules.  Dr. Rinne advised normally the resolution would have been referred to 
the Finance Comm ittee but instead the governing body has been asked to expedite the adoption 
process by suspension of rules to adopt at this meeting.  Mr. O’Donnell emphasized the tight time 
constraint and anticipated a SLIB meeting this Thursday , August 27 th, to consider the allocation 
model.  Upon inquiry, Mr. O’Donnell explained a hold harmless agreement would provide 
protection to the State of Wyoming so that they aren’t exposed to findings that funds weren’t spent 
correctly and would apply to all local governments .  He noted he is c onfident Governor Gordon 
would support the protection.  Gunnar Malm, Laramie County Commissioner, advised he was 
unaware of the resolution  and would like additional infor mation and discussion between 
community partners prior to considering whether or not to support the effort.  Amber Ash, DDA 
Executive Director, questioned how this will impact earmarked allocation for outside agencies in 
the City’s budget. Mr. O’Donnell confirmed the funding allocations for outside agencies such as 
the DDA were previously allocated.  He noted the City has gone before SLIB and have already 
been awarded grants.  Ms. Ash requested consideration of funding allocation by amendment in the 
City’s budget ordinance.  Dr. Rinne explained if th e resolution is  referred to the Finance 
Committee, there would be a 3 week delay as next week is a 5th week with no regular meetings of 
  
the governing body.  He reported there is the option to suspend rules which requires a motion and 
second and a ¾ affirmative vote of qualified members of the governing body. Upon inquiry, Kris 
Jones, City Clerk, advised in order to schedule a special meeting of the governing body, a press 
release would need to be issued 24 hours prior to the meeting to comply with public notification 
requirements.  Ms. Jones confirmed the resolution could be postponed to a special meeting of the 
governing body and noted a special Finance Committee meeting could also be scheduled prior to 
the special meeting of the governing body to consider a recommendation on the agenda item.    
Discussion continued involving proposed accountable method of a 50/50 split that would benefit 
the Laramie C ounty Commissioners and government and questions of what it would do for the 
City and municipalities , support of suspending rules  and the City Attorney’s expertise and 
significant amount of grant funding.  Mr. Laybourn moved to suspend rules.  Motion died due to 
lack of second.  Mr. Case moved to postpone to August 26, 2020 at 6:00 p.m. to a special meeting 
of the governing body, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. Shanor abstained from voting due to a work 
affiliation.  Support was expressed for scheduling a special meeting to allow for dialogue with the 
Laramie County Commissioners,  and it was noted that if the resolution is adopted, it would be 
ready in time for the August 27 th SLIB meeting.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present with the exception of Laybourn voting no. 
  
Preliminary Planned Unit Development (PUD) for The Enclave at Northgate, for Lot 8 and 
portions of Lots 6, 7, 9, 10, 11, and 12; Block 19; North Cheyenne, 2nd Filing; Cheyenne; Laramie 
County; Wyoming (located west of and  adjacent to Yellowstone Road, north of North Gate

Appointments of Jay Sullivan and Dr. Tracey Kinney to the Laramie County Juvenile

MEETING: Special Meeting of the Governing Body - Agenda Only | DATE: 2020-08-26 | CLIP_ID: 764 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 764

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
August 26, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in special session on this date beginning 
at 6:03 p.m. via electronic conference meeting .  Attending electronic conference meeting  were: 
COUNCIL PRESIDENT – Dr. Mark Rinne; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, 
Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Scott Royba l, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White. Absent: 
Mayor Marian J. Orr.  Also attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk. 
 
RESOLUTION – Supporting the State of Wyoming Governor’s proposal to have the State 
Loan and Investment Board consider modifying its rules to adopt an all ocation model for 
distribution of Coronavirus Aid, Relief, and Economic Security Act (CARES ACT) funding to 
local governments.  City Attorney Michael O’Donnell advised that due to concerns raised, it is 
recommended that the last four paragraphs (Whereas Clauses) on page 1 be removed  which 
involves funding distribution formulas.  Mr. O’Donnell suggested that all counties would rec eive 
an amount if they requested up -front funding.  Gunner Malm, Laramie County Commissioner, 
commented that Mr. O’Donnell’s comments relating to counties requesting up -front funding is 
most likely correct.  Mr. O’Donnell confirmed the city was set to rece ive $9 million. Comments 
were made that larger impacts have been felt by areas that are heavier populated concerning the 
pandemic.  Discussion included Governor Gordon’s distribution formula options and impacts, 
grant application submittals and approval pr ocess, and expenditure of funding timeline of 
December 30, 2020.  Mr. Case moved to remove the last four paragraphs on page 1 pertaining to 
the distribution formula, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr.  Shanor abstained from discussion and 
voting due to work affiliation with the Wyoming Department of Education.  Additional comments 
were made in regard to proposed funding split between cities and counties , timeline concerns, 
cooperation between municipalities, and support for a population -based distribution.  Motion to 
amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of 
Mr. Esquibel voting “no”.  Motion to adopt, as amended carried.   Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body present.   (#6084)             
 
RESOLUTION – Supporting the hiring of additional Park Maintenance full-time staff and 
seasonals and the pending re -appropriation of $244,000.00.  (General Fund) (SPONSORS – 
ROCKY CASE, BRIAN COOK, MIKE LUNA, AND JEFF WHITE) Mr. Case moved to suspend 
the ru les to adopt the resolution pursuant to State Statute §15 -1-115, seconded by Mr. Luna.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Mr. Case explained 
that he brought the resolution forward to mitigate on going maintenance issues with city 
maintained landscaping issues.  Mr. Case reported he has received numerous complaints from 
citizens and the proposal is to act quickly in an effort to save dying landscaping and prevent further 
decay.  Vicki Nemecek, Public Works Director, read into the record a statement relating to budget 
reductions through out all city departments resulting from the COVID -19 pandemic and lack of 
revenues.  Ms. Nemecek’s sta tement advised that all Department Directors worked as a team 
equally to provide necessary budget cuts and should continue to work as a team when considering 
rehiring needed staff.  She further reported that although some suffering public services can be 
seen such as brown grass and dying trees, there are many other areas that are being neglected due 
to the lack of adequate staffing and funding.  Ms. Nemecek requested consideration of maintaining 
an equitable balance with providing and adding back resources such as staffing and funding.  Mr. 
Case moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. Case moved to amend by striking “full time 
positions and ten seasonal” and replacing with “part time, temporary, or contract” throughout the 
resolution, and by striking “st riking all references to $224,000.00 and replacing it with 
$209,000.00, seconded by Mr. Luna.   Motion to amend carried.   Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body present.  Upon inquiry, Teresa Moore, Community Recreation and Events 
Director, explained that they could hire additional staff in a two-week time frame and would look 
at all options to achieve Mr. Case’s resolution objectives.  Upon inquiry, Robin Lockman, City 
Treasurer, provided information relating to reserves funding and noted she sent an email on August 
26th to members of the governing body which contain ed extensive budget and reserves funding 
information.  Mr. Shanor commented on support of addressing  landscaping maintenance and 
supported Ms. Nemecek’s comments involving a balanced a pproach for the city’s needs as a 
whole.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend by striking all references to $209,000.00 and replacing with 
$100,000.00, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Mr. Cook  voting “no”.  Main motion as twice 
amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#6085)                                   
               
 There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:51 p.m.  
 
 
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 
  
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  September 5, 2020 
 

---

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
September 14, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:07 p.m. via electronic conference meeting .  Attending electronic conference meeting  were: 
MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook (joining meeting at 
6:03 p.m.) , Ken Esquibel,  Pete Laybourn,  Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal,  Dicky 
Shanor (joining meeting at 6:03 p.m.) and Jeff White.  Also attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.   Upon request by Mr.  Laybourn, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Esquibel and Mr. White, agenda item #1 9 (a) was removed from the Consent 
Agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing  body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting 
“no”. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on August 24, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on August 26, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 Mayor Orr opened a public hearing for a transfer of ownership  for a resort liquor license 
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Omsai Hospitality, LLC d/b/a Red Lion and 
Conference Center of Cheyenne, 204 W. Fox Farm Rd., Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently 
held by Driftwood Special Servicing, LLC d/b/a Red Lion Hotel Cheyenne & Conference Center).  
Billie Addelman, attorney with Hirst Applegate representing the applicant, advised he was 
available for any questions. There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing 
having been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing and referred the item to Finance Committee. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 15.50, Historic 
Design Guidelines, of Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Case moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr.  Cook. Upon inquiry, Charles Bloom, Director of Planning and 
Development, explained the ordinance amendment request is on behalf of the Historic Preservation 
Board and notice of the amendment wasn’t mailed to adjacent property owners with the exception 
of a vacant property behind the Governor’s mansion.  Dr. Rinne explained the Historic Design 
Guidelines were created in 2004.  Mr. Bloom reported the ordinance amendment removes a step 
which currently requires changes or building permits to go to the Planning and Development  
  
office, and these will now go straight to the Histori c Preservation Board.  It was noted  the 
amendment is administrative and not substantive, and the public sector has expressed support for 
the amendment.  Mr. Bloom explained he is continuing to work with the City’s Attorney’s office 
related to what submittal type is necessary to go to the Historic Preservation Board and anticipates 
there will be a few minor ordinance changes  presented at the September 22 nd Public Services 
Committee meeting.  He further  noted there is no requirement to provide public notice. Upon 
inquiry, Mr. Bloom advised the proposed amendment  was in the Historic Preservation Board’s 
annual work plan they develop every year and mostly due to vacant property north of Governor’s 
mansion. Support for the ordinance was expressed.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.   
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming to create the Support Services Zone District and establishing design criteria 
– referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
  
[CA] RESOLUTION – Amending Resolution #3597, a Citizen Participation Plan, which 
establishes objectives, procedures, requirements, and responsibilities for the involvement of lower 
income individuals, as well as the community at large in the development of a City Comprehensive 
Five Year Housing and Community Development Plan .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
(#6086) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Accepting a grant in the amount of $150,000.00 from the Cheyenne 
Community Recreation District (Recreation Mill) for the replacement of the restroom/concession 
building at the Cheyenne Junior League Complex.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6087) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Creating a Special District for the ‘Golf, it’s the Least of our Problems’ 
Golf Tournament to be held on September 26, 2020, in which the possession of open containers is 
permitted within the Downtown District Boundaries .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
(#6088) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Approving water and sanitary sewer services, by outside user water and 
sanitary sewer services agreements, for SJT, LLC, for Tracts 21, 22, and 23, Green Subdivision, 
3rd Filing, situated in the SW¼SW¼ of Section 22, Township 14 North, Range 66 West, of the 6th

[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Buffalo
