Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located southeast of th e intersection of Hilltop Avenue 
and Bluff Place) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Providing for partial vacation of the Frontier North Final Plat – referred 
to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
 
 
  
[CA] RESOLUTION – Reappropriating the Fiscal Year 2021 Budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. 16 -4-112 & 16 -4-113 – referred to Finance Committee.  
(Sponsor – Mr. Luna) 
 
[CA] Preliminary Plat for Whitney Ranch, 4th Filing, a plat of a portion of the SE¼, Section 23, 
T14N, R66W, of the 6th PM, Laramie County, Wyoming (located northeast of the intersection of 
Dell Range Boulevard and Whitney Road) – referred to Public Services Committee. 
  
[CA] Volleyball Officiating Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Officials for Fun 
Club for the Community Recreation and Events 2020 -2021 season for youth and adult volleyball 
leagues (Recreation Programs Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7229) 
 
[CA] Notice of renewal of United States Department of  Agriculture Lease Number 57 -82HW-
1-0171/City Contract No. 150, between the United States of America, acting through the 
Agricultural Research Service, and the  City of Cheyenne for the lease of 1,998 acres of land 
known as the High Plains Grasslands Research Station, for the period of October 1, 2019 through 
September 30, 2020  (Revenue to City).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#150) 
 
[CA] Notice of renewal of United States Department of Agriculture Lease Number 57 -82HW-
1-0171/City Contract No. 150,  between the United States of America, acting through the 
Agricultural Research Service, and the  City of Cheyenne for the lease of 1,998 acres of land 
known as the High Plains Grasslands Research Station, for the period of October 1, 2020 through 
September 30, 2021 (Revenue to City).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#150) 
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne , Cheyenne Police 
Department, and Laramie County School District No. 1 for school resource officers for the 2020-
2021 school year and summer school 2021 (Revenue to City) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Metropolitan 
Planning Organization (MPO), and Summit Engineering for the East Park Greenway Underpass 
and Drainage Plan (MPO Federal Funding) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Contract Modification #2 to Contract #6933 between the City of Cheyenne and Vortex 
International Aquatic Play Solutions to upgrade ADA Visual Access Distinction and Construction 
(General Fund Reserves).  Ben Hornok, owner’s representative for the project, provided a detailed 
overview of the contract modification.   Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to 
approve in an amount not to exceed $4,345.00, seconded by Mr. Case.  Upon inquiry, Alessandra 
McCoy Fakelman, Deputy City Attorney, explained it is her understanding there is a $100,000.00 
voucher the City has not paid for the project because Vortex International Play Solutions  hasn’t 
completed everything on the project list.  Mr. Laybourn expressed concern relating to the project 
and noted that after a site visit, he was made aware of problems with the system, acknowledged 
how long and difficult this project has been and requested consideration to further review the issues 
prior to final payment to Vortex.  It was noted by Mr. Shanor that the item received a unanimous 
vote of support at the September 8th Finance Committee meeting and was eligible for the Consent 
Agenda.  Mr. Laybourn stated he voted in support at that meeting however, following that, became 
  
aware of the issues.  Mr. Hornok reported there is a current outstanding invoice balance amount of 
$143,343.00 and by approving this change order it would bring the total  due to $147,000.00.  He 
further supported that the City should have assurance the project is complete and the splash pad is 
functioning properly prior to final payment, noting he  is currently tracking  the progress of the 
project.  It was noted PH balance testing is occurring 3 times daily and there have been problems 
establishing acceptable levels  which triggers  the splash pad to shut down  if outside of those 
parameters.  Jason Sanchez, Community Recreation & Events Deputy Director, reported there are 
additional issues with the project within the UV water cleaning system and  advised he will not 
approve a final payment to Vortex until the splash pad is functioning properly.  It was further noted 
this modification is strictly related to ADA concerns.  Mr. Sanchez advised there have been issues 
from the onset primarily with the UV system. There have been fewer issues recently due to tech 
support received, however, faults over the weekend caused pumps to shut down. He wants to make 
sure the system is functioning appropriately before it is shut down for the winter.  Mr. Case raised 
a point of order as to discussion being germane to the modification before the governing body.  
Mayor Orr asked for clarification that the proposed modification is an additional expense above 
the original amount  and Mr. Hornok confirmed it was an additional expense.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes”—all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn and Mayor 
Orr voting “no”.  (#6933)   
 
 [CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #6981 between the City of Cheyenne and Plan 
One/Architects to extend the completion date for roof replacements at Fleet Maintenance, Parks 
Maintenance and Cheyenne Aquatics Center.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6981) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract # 6786 between the City of Cheyenne and Novo 
Benefits, LLC, to extend the term for an additional three (3) year period (General Fund) – referred 
to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract # 7195 between the City of Cheyenne and Simon 
Contractors for additional construction services for the Prairie Avenue and F rontier Mall Drive 
Intersection Project (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification # 2 to Contract # 6960 between the City of Cheyenne and Reiman 
Corporation for cost impacts associated with unforeseen site conditions, partial release of retainage 
and reduction of retainage from 10% to 5% to meet current state statutes for the Christensen 
Railroad Overpass & Corridor Project (Specific Purpose  Sales Tax) – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification # 2 to Contract # 6976 between the City of Cheyenne and Reiman 
Corporation for partial release of retainage and additional funds for final landscaping for the Evers 
Blvd. Reconstruction Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration to purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and Alamo Industrial, Seguin, 
TX, for the purchase of an Alamo Versa Pro Three Gang Flail Mower (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from Alamo Industrial, Sequin, TX, in an 
amount not to exceed $68,502.00). 
  
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #E -1-21 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and A1 
Organics for furnishing compost screening and gri nding services for the City of Cheyenne, 
Compost Facility (Solid Waste Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S -4-21 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Mid -
America Pool Renovation, Inc. for the Cheyenne Aquatic Center Main Pool Shell Resurfacing 
Project (2012 Specific Purpose Option Tax Overage Funds) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #S-5-21 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Academy 
Sports Turf, Inc. for the Powers  Field Synthetic Turf Replacement Project (Land and Water 
Conservation Grant and Recreation Programs Fund Player’s Fees) – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] Transfer of ownership for a retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s 
Office for Old Chicago Taproom II LLC d/b/a Old Chicago #74, 1734 Meadowland Drive, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held by Wadsworth Old Chicago Inc. d/b/a Old Chicago 
Restaurant.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 Under other business: 
 
 Mike O’Donnell, City Attorney, reported he has received concerns related to CARES Act 
funding and noted the City has been struggling to get approval of applications.  He advised the 
SLIB Board has set up a new system with an allocation system which replaces the first come first 
serve system and utilizes a formula to establish “coffee cans” which allocates money to individual 
municipal and community agencies.  Mr. O’Donnell reported the City’s allocation is currently $7 
million, noting that isn’t a guaranteed amount, just the amount the City can apply up to.  He further 
explained the application process is through the Office of State Land s and Investments  and the 
Attorney General’s office and that he has received guidance from State Lands related to the format 
for payroll reimbursements.  He explained the required information has been submitted twice and 
that he will submit again as requested relating to the new guidelines.  He recently rec eived 
information on how to apply for Personal Protective Equipment (PPE) and technology grants and 
that although this process is easier, it is still cumbersome.  It was explained that in order to acquire 
technology equipment, this requires a bidding proce ss that goes through the City’s purchasing 
policy procedures and must receive official support from the governing body (timeline and 
deadline is very tight).  Mr. O’Donnell explained he is working with outside agencies such as ARC 
and Safe Harbor related to their independent applications.  Upon inquiry, he reported there are 
options to approve bidding documents prior to the deadlines and  special meetings could be 
scheduled to expedite the process.  Mayor Orr commended the following City staff members for 
their diligent work on CARES Act funding options:  City Attorney Michael  O’Donnell, Shellie 
Colgan, Attorney’s office, TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing and Brendan Ames, Mayor’s office.   
 
 
  
 [Clerk’s note:  Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White vacated co mmittee room 
104 during Other Bus iness at approximately 6:45 p.m. and were absent for the remainder of the 
meeting] Mr. Laybourn echoed the Mayor’s appreciation to City staff working on the CARES Act 
funding application process.  Mr. Laybourn reported he h as concerns with the consent agenda 
process noting many items are considered to be routine items, however, he believes many of them 
should have full governing body discussion as well as allowing for  public comment.  He further 
commented that he believes it is important that the community is aware of these types of items and 
questioned the use of the consent agenda generally.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Orr advised there are  
issues related to public access  in returning to council chambers for public meetings , noting t he 
difficulty of meeting the 6 foot separation for social distancing guidelines  for members of the 
governing body on the dais.  Mr. O’Donnell confirmed council chambers is inadequate for meeting 
health guidelines.  He reported they have been working on sol utions to utilize the Civic Center 
however, current technology is insufficient to stream the meeting  and generally the facility can’t 
satisfy health guidelines.   He further advised the City was denied a recent funding a pplication 
request for the necessary equipment, however, the City plans to reapply.   
 
 Upon inquiry from Mr. Luna, Jason Sanchez, Community Recreation & Events Deputy 
Director, advised they recently received approval to hire additional part-time staff following the 
reduction in force to assist with park maintenance and have begun tending to neglected park areas.  
He further reporte d recently planted trees are being hand watered  while the weather allows and 
that numerous parks are receiving irrigation .  Upon inquiry from Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Sanchez 
further advised Cheyenne Frontier Days (CFD) volunteers have been assisting with maintenance 
of Pando Pond and provided information on required releases signed by Greenway volunteers for 
clean-up efforts.  Mayor Orr advised some clean -up requirements were expanded beyond trash 
pickup and involved use of machinery. 
 
 Upon inquiry from Mr. Esquibel, Mayor Orr advised warranty information will be provided 
to members of the City Council relating to the Splash Pad Project.   
 
 There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:50p.m.  
 
 
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
  September 23, 2020

MEETING: City Council Meeting - Agenda Only | DATE: 2020-09-28 | CLIP_ID: 767 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 767

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
September 28, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:07 p.m. via electronic conference meeting .  Attending electronic conference meeting  were: 
COUNCIL PRESIDENT – Dr. Mark Rinne; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, 
Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal , Dicky Shanor and Jeff 
White.  Absent: Mayor Marian J. Orr.  Also attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk. 
 
 Mr. Cook advised that as sponsor of agenda item #16, he wishes to withdraw the item at 
this time.  There being no objections, President Rinne advised th e item has been withdrawn . 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be routine 
items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Shanor, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Luna and Mr. Cook, agenda item # 13 was removed from the Consent Agenda.   Upon 
request by Mr. Shanor, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Luna and Mr. Cook, agenda item 
#18 (a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.   Mr. Luna moved to approve  remaining items, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing  body 
present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on September 14, 2020.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on September 21, 2020.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 15.50, Historic 
Design Guidelines, of Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming.  Milward Simpson, member of Cheyenne Historic Preservation Board (HPB), advised 
he was available for any questions  and noted he has worked with the Planning & Dev elopment 
Department on the proposed changes that will provide efficiency and simplify the appeals process.  
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Case moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr.  Cook.  Mr. Case moved to amend by substitute dated September 18, 2020, 
seconded by Mr . White .  Charles Bloom , Planning & Development Director, explained the 
amendment clarifies subsections where it may be  interpreted that a full building permit be 
submitted for review by the  HPB. Previously expressed concerns were reiterated relating to the 
lack of notification of adjacent property owners.   Upon inquiry, Mr. Bloom and Mr. Simpson 
advised they have not heard or been made aware of any comments or concerns from members of 
the public in the last 2 weeks regarding this proposal.  Further comments related adjacent property 
owner notification were  expressed noting that due to the current electronic meeting structure 
   
caused by the COVID pandemic, many people may not be aware of the proposed changes, 
therefore, they would not have offered comments/concerns.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Laybourn advised 
he has not spo ken to affected property owners . Supportive comments  were made  relating to 
proposed changes and appreciation was expressed to Mr. Simpson and Mr. Bloom for their work 
on the proposed changes.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  Main motion as amended carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body  present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn 
voting “no”.  [Clerk’s note:  Dr. Rinne advised as acting Chair, he would not be casting votes 
unless it would be to break a tie vote] (#4325)   
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming to create the Support Services Zone District and establishing design 
criteria.  Charles Bloom, Planning & Development Director, reported the purpose of the 
amendment is to create a new zone district in response to F .E. Warren Air Force Base proposed 
development on the edge of base property accessible to the public.  He advised that after reviewing 
the current Unified Development Code (UDC) it was  discovered there isn’t a use that would 
support military operations.  He noted the proposed amendments are a blend of several districts 
that are more practical for developers to utilize.  Mr. Bloom advised this is a major change that is 
proposed to create a unique  lot type t hat allows up to 90% lot coverage , has no set back 
requirements and can be built to property lines.  He reviewed proposed design criteria including: 
parking requirements, signage, multi-family requirements, commercial development standards and 
material requirements, big box building requirements, alternative equivalent option and a reduction 
in living landscape materials requirement to help with water conservation. It was noted that 
approximately 2 weeks ago this was presented by Brendan Ames, City’s Chief Econ omic 
Development Officer, to members of the military of which who seemed receptive of the plan.  Mr. 
Case moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Chapter 10.44, Weight Limits, of Title 10, 
Vehicles and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to 
Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Chapter 5.04, Business Licenses Generally, 
amending Chapter 5.08, Business License Fees, and creating a new Chapter 5.77, Nicotine Product 
Sales, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations; amending Chapter 8.64, Smokin g in Public 
Places, of Title 8, Health and Safety; and amending Chapter 9.16, Offenses By or Against Minors, 
of Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the Municipal Code of the  City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
– referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsors – Dr. Rinne and Mr. White) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Buffalo 
Ridge Estates, Eleventh Filing, a replat of all of Lot 15, Block 21, Buffalo Ridge Estates, 
Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located southeast o f the intersection of Hilltop Avenue 
and Bluff Place).  Casey Palma, Steil Surveying and agent for the owner, advised the final plat was 
defeated in February 2020 by a tie vote and is before the governing body again with minor changes.  
Mr. Palma reported past concerns were related to smaller proposed lot sizes than the surrounding 
   
neighborhood and street facing garage placement.  He advised concerns were reviewed and it was 
decided they would add a provision that would require side or alley loadin g garages that would 
prevent drivers backing onto Hil ltop.  Mr. Palma provided additional comments relating to lot 
sizes in relation to surrounding neighborhood  and previous county developments with required 
larger lot sizes due to septic system requirements.  It was noted that proposed lot width is identical 
to those on the west side of Hilltop.  Mr. Palma stated the proposed residential units would provide 
affordable housing prices as lower prices and smaller are desired for the community.  Additionally, 
he advised this is a p ositive infill project that will raise median home cost s in the neighborhood 
and the plat meets United Development Code (UDC) requirements in its entirety.  Upon inquiry, 
Mr. Palma provided additional lot size footprint information relating to total lot square footage and 
single-family versus duplex residential structure styles.  Mr. Case moved to adopt, seconded by 
Mr. Roybal.  Dr. Rinne, Council President and acting chairman, relinquished duties of the Chair 
to Mr. White, Vice President, on this item for the purpose of being able to discuss the proposal.  
Dr. Rinne stated that proposed lots are 1/3 smaller than they are in the neighborhood and it’s hard 
to imagine 2 houses  fitting on that lot size .  He doesn’t think the proposed smaller lot size d 
residential units would benefit the neighborhood and is opposed to duplexes in this area.  Dr. Rinne 
noted this is similar to a project that was previously turned down in Ward 3.  Support was expressed 
for the project, infill developments and affordable housing.   Kris Jones, City Clerk, provided 
clarification regarding staff conditions relating to the Preliminary Plat on the agenda.   Mr. Cook 
moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  It was noted for the record 
that Mr. White as Acting Chairman for this item, did not cast a vote.  (#6091)   
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Providing for partial vacation of the Frontier North Final Plat .  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#6092) 
 
 RESOLUTION – Reappropriating the Fiscal Year 2021 Budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. 16 -4-112 & 16-4-113.  Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded 
by Mr. Laybourn.  Concern was expressed related to the resolution’s accompanying memorandum 
from Robin Lockman, City Treasurer, regarding general funds for a new Police Crime Analyst 
position, asking Laramie County School District #1 for an additional $80,000.00 and the fulltime 
employee cap .  Upon inquiry,  Robin Lockman, City Treasurer, noted that the Ice & Events 
Maintenance Technician position wasn’t included in the budget and was one of the positions that 
was not rehired when vacated due to the hiring freeze.  Comment was made related to concern 
over filling a Recreation Specialist position at a higher rate than the maintenance technician and 
was not included in the FY 2021 budget.  Upon inquiry related to the Crime Lab Analyst position 
and in relation to the fulltime employee (FTE) cap, Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, advised 
he provided information at the recent Finance Committee meeting that there are additional budget 
funds for the position, however, they do not plan on hiring the position  and funding will go                                                              
towards next year’s budget.  Chief Kozak stated that due to uncertainty resulting from the COVID 
pandemic and possible future school closures , the city may not have to provide school resource 
officer services all year .  Ms. Lockman reported  the resolution adds the LCSD #1 revenue of 
$80,000.00 to the Police budget and it can be held in the Professional Services line item and noted 
the memorandum issued with the reappropriation is informational only, not a binding document.  
She further noted she can provide an updated memorandum if desired to reflect the discussion on 
   
this item.  Upon further inquiry, Ms. Lockman reported the resolution does not authorize the hiring 
of the position and the position could be filled if approved as the city would exceed the FTE cap.  
Michael O’Donnell, City Attorney, confirmed that the FTE cap remains in place by ordinance and 
the resolution i s only explanatory and doesn’t increase the FTE cap.  He further explained  the 
Chief has stated the position will not be filled but desires the opportunity to create the position.  It 
was noted that in order to increase the FTE cap, the ordinance would need to come back before 
the governing body to be amended.  Chief Kozak report ed that at the onset of the COVID 
pandemic, LCSD#1 was not billed when schools closed for resource officers as they weren’t in the 
schools, however, LCSD#1 felt obligated to honor the previous agreement and continued to pay 
the city.  Upon inquiry, Jason  Sanchez, Community Recreation & Events Deputy Director, 
reported that prior to the pandemic they had lost a programmer and maintenance technician at the 
Ice & Event s Center. They feel the programmer position is important for  success of the  
establishment and won’t increase FTE numbers but will be backfilling a position.  Mr. Sanchez 
and Ms. Lockman advised the position swap revenue can be made neutral by waiting a month to 
fill it along with other budget adjustments.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend by adding the f ollowing 
footnote to the resolution:  Footnote 1 -Passage of this resolution is not an endorsement of the 
memorandum dated September 9, 2020 from City Treasurer Robin Lockman to Mayor Orr and 
members of the City Council, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. Shanor st ated he believes the 
amendment makes it clear that there has been expressed consternation related to the memorandum. 
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” all members of the governing body present with the 
exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend by deleting the Ice & Events 
Center row and adjusting totals accordingly , seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion to amend carried.  
Voting “yes”—all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn 
voting “no”.  Main motion as twice amended carried.  Voting “yes”—all member of the governing 
body present.  (#6093)                                      
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Amending Resolution #6058, the City of Cheyenne Comprehensive 
Consolidated Plan 2020-2024 and 2020 Annual Action Plan, prepared by the Cheyenne Housing 
and Community Development Office – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Luna) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Certifying the Whitney Road Corridor Plan – referred to Public Services 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Supporting and recommending the United States Congress propose and 
send to the states for ratification, an amendment to the United States Co nstitution clarifying the 
distinction between the rights of natural persons and the rights of corporations, unions, and other 
legal entities, and providing congress and the state legislatures the right to regulate monies raised 
and spent for political purposes – this item removed from agenda at sponsor’s request. 
 
 Preliminary Plat for Whitney Ranch, 4th Filing, a plat of a portion of the SE¼, Section 23, 
T14N, R66W, of the 6th PM, Laramie County, Wyoming (located northeast of the intersection of 
Dell Range Boulevard and Whitney Road) .  Mr. Case moved to acknowledge the Planning 
Commission’s recommendation to approve with conditions 1 a, b, c and d, 2 and 3, seconded by 
Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the 
exception Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.   
   
  Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne , Cheyenne Police 
Department, and Laramie County School District No. 1 for school resource officers for the 2020-
2021 school year and summer school 2021 (Revenue to City).  Chief Kozak advised negotiations 
were made with LCSD#1 who requested the following  changes: ability to flex out officer ’s 
schedules, if an officer is absent more than 5 days the city will replace the officer, LCSD#1 is not 
required to pay for professional training and if an officer is pulled for an emergency for half a day 
they don’t want to pay for the whole day.  Chief Kozak advised he brought up the charge of base 
salary for officers because of officer experience and he requested LCSD#1 pay for actual salary 
and benefits for 75% of year to which they agreed.  He further noted the city pays for insurance, 
uniforms, equipment, etc.  Mr. White moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Laybourn. Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” all members present. (#7238) 
 
[CA] Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Metropolitan

[CA] Transfer of ownership for a resort liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s

carried. 
 
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented. 
 
 
   
 Report of Community Facility Fees Fiscal Year 2019/2020 pursuant to Section 4.2.5 of the 
Unified Development Code (UDC).   Charles Bloom, Planning & Development Director, reported 
these are park impact fees the city has received and includes public infrastructure fees received.  
Mr. Bloom provided additional information including:  The $400.00 fee is a common amount that 
is charged with single family homes, there are 2 different ways to collect fees with building permits 
and also at time of subdivision  and developer is required to bring in park acquisition fees which 
could vary in price as these are redistributed and used for the particular development.  Mr. Luna 
moved to acknowledge receipt of report, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” - 
all members of the governing body present. 
 
 There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:19 p.m.

MEETING: City Council Meeting - Agenda Only | DATE: 2020-10-12 | CLIP_ID: 769 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 769

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
October 12, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:06 p.m. via electronic conference meeting .  Attending electronic conference meeting  were: 
MAYOR – Marian J. Orr (joining meeting at 6:08 p.m.) ; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, 
Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky 
Shanor and Jeff White.   Also attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  [Clerk’s note: Due to technical 
difficulties, Dr. Rinne, Council President, Chaired agenda items 1 through 5, relinquishing duties 
to Mayor Orr at 6:08 p.m. during discussion of agenda item 6.]  
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. )  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Luna.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of 
Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on September 28, 2020.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming to create the Support Services Zone District and establishing design criteria.  
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Case moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr . Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
(#4326) 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Chapter 10.44, Weight Limits, of Title 10, 
Vehicles and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Case moved 
to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Cook. It was noted that Police Chief Brian Kozak 
had contacted local trucking companies and had received a response .  Based on the response he 
would like to postpone the ordinance for further review.  Dr. Rinne moved to postpone to October 
26, 2020, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter 5.04, Business Licenses Generally, 
amending Chapter 5.08, Business License Fees, and creating a new Chapter 5.77, Nicotine Product 
Sales, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations; amending Chapter 8.64, Smoking in Public 
Places, of Title 8, Health and Safety; and amending Chapter 9.16, Offenses By or Against Minors, 
of Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheye nne, Wyoming.  
Nicholas Vaughn commented in support of the ordinance and stated he works in education and has 
   
seen a rise in tobacco use in youth.  He believes enforcing the purchase age of 21+ can help deter 
addiction to nicotine at a young age.  Brittany Wardle, Cheyenne Regional Medical Center, advised 
the ordinance change aligns with federal and state laws  and effective t obacco retail licensing 
prevents illegal tobacco sales.  She added that an e ffective licensing system includes fee s, the 
option to suspend/revoke a license and the ordinance changes allow for local enforcement and will 
help curb nicotine addiction in the community.  Mr. Case advised there was n o recommendation 
from Public Services Committee.   Mr. Case moved to approve on second re ading, seconded by 
Mr. Roybal.  Mr. White moved to amend page 2, section 2, in the second paragraph by striking the 
“one hundred twenty-five dollars” license fee and replacing with “sixty-five dollars” and making 
the same change to the numerical portion in parenthesis, seconded by Mr. Case.  Mr. White advised 
the fee reduction is to alleviate cost increase concerns , noting it will keep the cost the same as it 
currently is.  Opposition to the amendment was voiced noting the increase is equivalent to $5.00 a 
month.  Comments were made related to the effects of nicotine addiction and the affiliated costs 
to society.  Mayor Orr advised, upon inquiry, the City does not fund research.  Mr. White reported 
the cost increase was proposed and intend ed to help with expenses associated with compliance 
checks performed by the Police Department.  Robin Lockman, City Treasurer, upon inquiry, 
advised that currently the fees for tobacco licenses go into the general fund , however , the 
Governing Body can request  the Treasurer’s Office to appropriate the  revenue annually to the 
Police Department.  Discussion continued on poor timing of  increasing regulatory fines on small 
businesses, ordinance changes creating an additional layer of administrative fees and compliance 
with state and federal laws.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body with the exception of Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mayor Orr and Dr. Rinne v oting “no”.  
Main motion as amended carried . Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the 
exception of Mr. Luna, Mayor Orr and Mr. Shanor voting “no”.   
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Section 8.56.070, Loudspeakers, Sound Cars, 
and Sound Trucks, of Chapter 8.56, Noise Control, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Municipal 
Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. 
Case) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from HR (High -Density Residential) to CB 
(Community Business) for property generally located east of Interstate 25 and north of Central 
Avenue adjacent to Hynds Boulevard – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. 
Case) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of

Business) for property generally located north of West Lincolnway at Grant Avenue – referred to

ORDINANCE –1st READING – Vacating the entirety of the Stinson Avenue right-of-way

of West Lincolnway Highway – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsors – Mr. Case, Mr.  
Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White)   
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to

MEETING: City Council Meeting - Agenda Only | DATE: 2020-10-26 | CLIP_ID: 771 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 771

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
October 26, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:06 p.m. via electronic conference meeting .  Attending electronic conference meeting  were: 
COUNCIL PRESIDENT – Dr. Mark Rinne; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, 
Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna,  Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Absent: 
Mayor Marian Orr.  Also attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk. The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed  with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. )  Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. White and Mr. Luna, agenda items #14 & #21 (c) were removed from the Consent 
Agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on October 12, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on October 19, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Chapter 5.04, Business Licenses Generally, 
amending Chapter 5.08, Business License Fees, and creating a new Chapter 5.77, Nicotine Product 
Sales, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations; amending Chapter 8.64, Smoking in Public 
Places, of Title 8, Health and Safety; and amending Chapter 9.16, Offenses By or Against Minors, 
of Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming .  
Joe Chenchar, Assistant City Attorney, provided a staff report stating the ordinance will increase 
the legal age to purchase nicotine to 21 which aligns code with state and federal law.  He provided 
the following information on the ordinance: application fee will remain the same, soft  drink 
licensing requirement will be r emoved, r egulatory provisions have been added  giving the 
governing body the ability to suspend or revoke a license and the fines for violating city code have 
been increased.   Brittany Wardle, Cheyenne Regional Medical Center , voiced support for the 
ordinance, reiterated that increasing the legal age to purchase nicotine to  21 aligns with state and 
federal law s, noted the ordinance provides for authority and enforcement for  police officers , 
licensing prevents illegal sales and provides structure so businesses comply and advised the effects 
of nicotine use is the leading cause of preventable death in the United States.   Mr. White moved 
to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Dr. Rinne relinquished duties of the 
Chair to Mr. Luna.  Dr. Rinne advised the ordinance brings code into compliance with state and 
federal guidelines, allows for removal of license after four (4) offenses, and stated he believes the 
   
fines should be onerous.  He further commented on: businesses selling to minors, state and federal 
laws and nicotine being the most addictive drug  contributing to disease and deaths.  Dr. Rinne 
stated he does not see the proposed changes to be devastating to small businesses, noting most 
businesses selling nicotine are large corporations.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Chenchar reported that if an 
alleged offender p leads not guilty , they will get a trial date  and further advised in that case, he 
would represent the city and the officer issuing the citation would be the witness.  Upon further 
inquiry, Mr. Chenchar advised Municipal Court minor infractions are being tried and prosecuted 
currently and that maybe some cases in higher courts are not currently being tried due to COVID-
19.  Mr. Chenchar further advised  the fines would be the responsibility of the citation holder, 
noting the issuing officer would have discretion to issue to either the individual employee or 
business owner.  Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, advised it is his understanding that some 
low-level misdemeanor crimes are being dismissed at the Circuit Court level  and that infractions 
relating to this ordinance will be at the municipal level.  Concerns were raised relating to citing 
employees who may be financially s truggling and  local bar owners who have been damaged 
exponentially by COVID-19 by adding more financial burden.  It was further noted the proposed 
increase in administrative fees are not required by state statute and would be an economic 
devastation when currently 1in 5 businesses are closing down.  Further comments were made in 
support of the ordinance amendments as businesses should be following the age requirement of 21 
to purchase nicotine and that most businesses selling nicotine products are big box c orporations 
not small businesses.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present 
with the exception of Mr. Case, Mr. Luna, and Mr. Shanor voting “no”.   
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Chapter 10.44, Weight Limits, of Title 10, 
Vehicles and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. (Postponed from 
October 12, 2020).  Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, reported the proposed amendment will 
provide for updating the current ordinance  for the purpose of helping to prevent unnecessary 
damage to residential streets from heavy semi-truck traffic on smaller less traveled streets and will 
allow for enforcement.  He advised the amendment would limit the weight l oad permissible on 
residential streets and that current language does not provide for these regulations, noting the heavy 
loads damage infrastructure and bridges  and they require more time to stop.  He provided the 
following information relating to the pro posed substitute: change from complete prohibition of 
heavy truck travel to allow for trucks that have scheduled stops  (semis can be on streets if they 
have a scheduled delivery), provides a Schedule H for load restrictions, applies to certain weights 
and noted delivery trucks such as Amazon won’t be impacted.  Chief Kozak reported he did not 
want to have a standard that was different than federal and state laws and explained companies 
that have a need to go over federal and state law weight limits can do so by obtaining a permit.  He 
further noted that trucks that can drive on highways without a permit can also drive on city streets 
without a permit and that the city will allow overweight permits for divisible loads .  It was noted 
the Police Department Traffic Unit will be trained in weight enforcement if  the ordinance is 
approved and that cause must exist before a truck can be weighed.   Upon inquiry, Chief Kozak 
explained he plans to purchase portable scales with drug cartel money.  He advised current truck 
routes will be maintained as listed in Schedule I and the city will allow 117,000 pounds without a 
permit on truck routes  which are designed for heavier loads.   He reported a public meeting was 
held with the trucking industry last week and all of their recommendations have been included in 
the proposed substitute amendment.  It was noted that one heavy load has the same impact as 9,000 
   
cars, pavement is designed to last 20 years at the 80,000 lb. level and that heavy trucks can wear 
pavement out in 7 years.   He reported the proposed permits will be intr oduced by resolution .  
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Case moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr.  Roybal.  Dr. Rinne relinquished Chair to Mr. White.  Chief Kozak advised he 
emailed the proposed substitute to interested parties on Friday, October 23rd.  He noted there were 
approximately 30 people from trucking industry  who attended the meeting on Tuesday, October 
20th with city staff and the primary area of concern was there were streets on Schedule H that they 
didn’t want on the schedule and  those have been removed .  Chief Kozak reported he r eceived 
feedback that permits are reasonable and contain every possible use and that the proposed fees are 
fair.  He noted he has not received any feedback on the proposed substitute.   Dr. Rinne moved to 
amend by substitute dated October 26, 2020, seconded by Mr. Case.  Support was voiced for the 
substitute ordinance and comment was made that the primary concerns voiced at the meeting with 
the trucking industry were Deming Drive, Parsley Boulevard and Ames Ave.   Anissa Gerard, 
Traffic Engineer, explained the streets with concern have been removed from Schedule H.  Kris 
Jones, City Clerk, confirmed the ordinance is on second reading.  Comments were made regarding 
importance of protecting city streets from damage and that  the proposed substitute i ncorporates 
feedback from the trucking industry.  Upon inquiry, Chief Kozak provided details relating to the 
proposed weight changes and advised those weights are consistent with state and federal 
requirements.  Chief Kozak advised a resolution would be necessary to set permit fees and that the 
intent is for the revenue to go toward enforcement efforts from the Police Department.  Discussion 
included concerns regarding equal enforcement for locals and out of state truckers and uncertainty 
regarding substance of proposed substitute.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” all members 
of the governing body  present with the exception of Mr. Case and Mr. Cook voting “ no”.  
Comments were made that although effected parties were involved with the process, it is not the 
appropriate time to add more regulations, permitting fees, and fines on  small businesses.  
Discussion included importance of protecting streets from unnecessary deterioration, open 
discussions held with the trucking industry  and anticipated additional feedback on proposed 
substitute.  Mr. Luna noted he received messages that industry members can’t access tonight’s 
Zoom meeting.   Rick Fierro, Ram Trucking Inc., advised he and his colleagues were under the 
impression the ordinance wasn’t going to be before the city council tonight.  Mr. Laybourn moved 
to postpone to November 9, 2020, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Katie Legerski, Executive Director for 
Wyoming Contractors Association, advised they have not had ample time to review the proposed 
substitute.  Ms. Jones, as a point of clarification, provided the timeline moving forward should the 
ordinance be postponed as follows: ordinance to Public Services Committee on November 3rd and 
November 17 th, and  to the City Council meeting on November 9 th with final approval on 
November 23rd.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present.  Upon inquiry by Ms. Jones, it was clarified that Mr. Luna and Mr. White did vote while 
chairing items #7 and #8 and that Dr. Rinne would only vote if it would affect the outcome while 
chairing the remaining agenda items.     
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Section 8.56.070, Loudspeakers, Sound Cars, 
and Sound Trucks, of Chapter 8.56, Noise Control, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Municipal 
Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Alessandra McCoy Fakelman, Deputy City Attorney, 
advised the purpose of the amendment is to change written permission for scenarios related to the 
ordinance from the mayor to the police chief and scenarios will require a permit in accordance 
   
with Section 8.56.060 of city code.  She reported a copy of the written permission shall be kept on 
file in the City Clerk’s office outlining the parameters of the approval.  She noted compliance is 
required with current noise provisions of city code.  Mr. Case moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. Case, as sponsor, advised the purpose is to align the code with existing 
noise related city code requirements.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from HR (High -Density Residential) to CB 
(Community Business) for property generally located east of Interstate 25 and north of Central 
Avenue adjacent to Hynds Boulevard.  Mr. Case moved to approve on second reading, seconded 
by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the C ity of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification of Lot 1, Block 2 and Lot 1, Block 3, Back 40 
Subdivision Second Filing from MUB (Mixed -Use Business Emphasis) to CB (Community 
Business) for property generally located north of West Lincolnway at Grant Av enue.  Mr. Case 
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr . Luna. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all

ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Vacating the entirety of the Stinson Avenue right-of-way 
lying between Blocks 404 and 410, Original City, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming and south 
of West Lincolnway Highway.  Mr. Case moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. 
White.  Mr. Case moved to amend by substitute map dated October 9, 2020, seconded by Mr . 
White.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
define, establish permissibility, and create Specific Use Standards for the use ‘Emergency Shelter’. 
Mr. Case moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
   
 RESOLUTION – Authorizing the submittal of a request to the Wyoming State Loan and 
Investment Board to amend Drinking Water State Revolving Fund Loan #133 from $4.9 million 
to $7.4 million for the City of Cheyenne Hydroelectric Generation Facility Project and authorizing 
the Mayor, City Clerk, and the Director of the Board of Public Utilities to sign all loan documents.  
Mr. Shanor abstained.  Mr. Luna moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. White.  Clint Bassett , Water 
Treatment Manger (BOPU), reported  BOPU is requesting the amendment to increase  the loan 
amount with a 0% interest rate.  Mr. Bassett provided information on the loan and project stating 
the funding is for design of the Hydroelectric Generation Facility  Project which started in 2013  
and feasibility and economic studies have been completed.  Mr. Basset explained the project went 
out to bid in May and the range of received bids were from $ 5.3 million to $7 million and they 
weren’t able to fund the project and had to reject all bids.  He advised some factors relating to the 
higher bids were a busy bid ding time, labor shortage in  the construction industry driving up 
construction prices and noted they have added components to the project.  He further reported they 
   
plan to rebid the project in D ecember 2020 and they will be s eparating project components that 
can be completed at a later date.  Mr. Bassett further explaine d the loan repayment will increase 
and the project will need to be supplemented by water revenue.  Comments were made regarding 
the importance of the project, providing public awareness of such projects relating to the consent 
agenda process and wildfires and droughts involving the BOPU.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body present.  (#6097) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for

 Consideration of Bid #E -2-21 for furnishing one (1) new 2020 -2021 patch truck for the 
City of Cheyenne Street and Alley Department (2015 -2018 1% Sales Tax).  Jeff Brown, Floyd’s 
Truck Center, advised they submitted 1 bid with 2 different body companies  which met 100% of 
   
the required specifications.  Mr. Brown shared past frustrations experienced with the city’s bidding 
process.  Mr. Luna moved to accept bid from Floyd’s Truck Center, Cheyenne, in an amount not 
to exceed $207,063.00, seconded by Mr. White.  Upon inquiry, Craig LaVoy, Deputy Director of 
Public Works, advised they received 3 bids and that the low bidder was Floyd’s Truck Center and 
that staff recommends approval.  Mr. LaVoy reported the truck would serve as an additional patch 
truck for repairing city streets which will hopefully allow the department to accomplish twice the 
number of repairs.  Discussion included : importance of maintaining the city’s road system, 
inquiries as how to fund additional staff members to perform repairs  and efforts of the Public 
Works Department to provide adequate maintenance.  Upon inquiry, Mr. LaVoy advised he would 
be providing members of the governing body  a plan for ad ding staff for a second patch crew.  
Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.

 Under other business:  
 
   
 Brenda Roper comment ed on and requested clarification on why Felix Pino’s name was 
removed from the sign at transfer station. She advised that she spoke with Vicki Nemecek, Public 
Works Director, who explained the sign would be replaced.  Ms. Roper further requested Felix 
Pino’s name be added to the new sign.  Comments were made by Council members who stated 
they had received calls on the issue and that the sign will be replaced with a new sign.  It was noted 
the community supports Mr. Pino’s name on the sign and it was clarified  the new sign will have 
the city logo and identifies the building as the Tr ansfer Station.  It was further noted  “Felix Pino 
Transfer Station” is on the building .  Craig LaVoy, Deputy Director of Public Works,  reported 
there was a loss of communication and the sign was removed as it was hard to see , and the sign 
will be replaced and will contain Felix Pino’s name.    
 
 There bein g no fur ther business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:48 p.m.  
 
 
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting - Agenda Only | DATE: 2020-11-09 | CLIP_ID: 773 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 773

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
November 9, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:06 p.m. via electronic conference meeting .  Attending electronic conference meeting  were: 
MAYOR - Marian Orr, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete 
Laybourn, Mike Luna,  Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also 
attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk. The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. )  Mr. Luna moved to approv e, seconded by Mr. White.   
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing  body with the exception of Mr. 
Laybourn voting “no”.  [Kris Jones, City Clerk, reported for the record that Dr. Rinne joined the 
meeting 6:01 p.m. prior to the vote on Consent Agenda] 
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on October 26, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending Section 8.56.070, Loudspeakers, Sound Cars, 
and Sound Trucks, of Chapter 8.56, Noise Control, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Municipal 
Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Case 
moved to approve on thir d and final reading, seconded by Mr.  Roybal. Motion carried. Voting 
“yes”- all members of the governing body. (#4328) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from HR (High -Density Residential) to CB 
(Community Business) for property generally located east of Interstate 25 and north of Central 
Avenue adjacent to Hynds Boulevard.  Mr. Case moved to approve on third  and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
(#4329) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd  READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification of Lot 1, Block 2 and Lot 1, Block 3, Back 40 
Subdivision Second Filing from MUB (Mixed -Use Business Emphasis) to CB (Community 
Business) for property generally located nort h of West Lincolnway at Grant Avenue.  Mr. Case 
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body. (#4330) 
 
 
   
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Vacating the entirety of the Stinson Avenue right-of-way 
lying between Blocks 404 and 410, Original City, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming and south 
of West Lincolnway Highway.  Mr. Case moved to approve on third  and final reading, seconded 
by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4331) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
define, establish permissibility, and create Specific Use Standards for the use ‘Emergency Shelter’. 
Mr. Case moved to approve on third  and final reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4332) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter 10.44, Weight Limits, of Title 10, 
Vehicles and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. (Postponed from 
October 26, 2020). Sheila Foertsch, Katie Legerski, Executive Director for Wyoming Contractors, 
Association, Mike Vaughn, Vaughn Concrete Products  and Rick Fierro, Ram Trucking, voiced 
support for postponing or tabling the ordinance voicing concerns involving:  time to evaluate 
proposed amendments, limitations on delivery of products and loss of revenue to members of the 
trucking industry.  Chief Brian Kozak, Cheyenne Police Department, advised trucks that can drive 
on highways without a permit can also drive on city streets without a permit and that the city will 
allow overweight permits for divisible loads.  He reported t he change would be a burden to the 
Police Department Traffic Unit as they will be trained in weight enforcement and that cause must 
exist before a truck can be weighed.  Chief Kozak also noted the department has had multiple 
meetings with the trucking industry.  Mr. Case moved to approve on second reading, seconded by 
Dr. Rinne.  Mr. Case moved to amend by substitute titled “November 3, 2020 amendment to the 
October 26, 2020 amendment”, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing bod y.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend Section 10.44.70 by deleting 
the last sentence and replacing it with “Wyoming State Statute 31 -18-802 with Tables 2 and 2, 
seconded by Mr. Luna.  Discussion included potential postponement and concerns involving 
including statutes as they can be amended.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend Section 10.44.060 line two by adding the term 
“overweight”, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion to amend failed due to a tie vote.  Voting “yes” – 
Mr. Cook, Mayor Orr, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. 
Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna and Mr. Shanor.  Dr. Rinne moved to postpone to November 
23, 2020, seconded by Mr. Ro ybal.  Discussion continued relating to the following concerns and 
topics:  Additional time requested to consider and evaluate proposed amendments, compliance 
with State and Federal regulations, enforcement, option s to table or postpone ordinance  and 
protecting longevity and integrity of city streets .  Kris Jones, City Clerk, provided clarification 
relating to potential postponement timelines.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body.                 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Section 2.84.070, Board of Adjustment, of

[CA] Minimum Revenue Guaranteed Payment Agreement between the City of Cheyenne,

$120,175.00) (#7254) 
 
Memorandum of Understanding between EQ Capital , LLC, and the City of Cheyenne to 
build a technology ecosystem in Wyoming focused on investing in tech startups.  Brandon Ames, 
Chief Economic Development Officer, provided details of the Memorandum of Understanding  
(MOU).  Mr. Ames advised the city bares no fi nancial responsibility relating to the MOU , that 
Cheyenne was chosen due to the proximity to Denver, CO. and the purpose is to help startup 
companies in Cheyenne.  He reported the intent is to partner with the city to create business 
opportunities noting EQ Capital, LLC was instrumental in the development of companies such as 
Door Dash and Air BNB.  Governing body members requested consideration to postpone and to 
have a City Council work session to learn more about the opportunity and long-range expectations 
of the MOU.   Mr. Ames advised the owners of EQ Capital, LLC are currently in route to Cheyenne 
and he would speak with  them regarding meeting with members of the g overning body.    
Discussion included the desire to learn more regarding the agreement and the exciting possibilities 
   
for Cheyenne.     Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Luna moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. 
White.  Mr. Laybourn moved to postpone to November 23, 2020, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Motion 
to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.

Consideration of Bid #E -7-21 for Ice Control Product for the City of Cheyenne Street &

reported that although there are alternative products such as sand, the Ice Slicer product eliminates 
broken windshields and the need to sweep streets follow ing snow events.  Upon inquiry, Ms. 
Nemecek advised the city has used this product for approximately 15 years.  Concerns were voiced

 Consideration of Bid # S-2-21 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Casper 
Electric, Inc., for the 24th / 25th / Carey / Pioneer Traffic Signal Upgrade Project.  (2015-2018 1% 
Sales Tax).  Kris Jones, City Clerk, advised this item was inadvertently left off of Consent Agenda.  
This item was referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 Consideration of Bid #S-8-21 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and JTL Group 
Inc. d/b/a Knife River, for the 2020 Miscellaneous Concrete Project.  (2015-2018 & 2019-2022 
1% Sales Tax).  Kris Jones, City Clerk, advised that this item was inadvertently left off of Consent

 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
Appointments of Kaylee Ashley, David Barker, Ty Bronder, Garrison Cook, Ashen Doty, 
Makiya Green, Neveah Green, Emily Lucero, Zoey Lundin and Elizabeth Stump to the 2020-2021 
Mayor’s Youth Council.  Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing  body with the exception of Mayor Orr  (Mayoral 
appointments).   
 
 Presentation of the Annual DDA Budget Report.  Amber Ash, DDA Director, presented a 
year to date comprehensive report on the DDA annual budget.  Ms. Ash provided details relating 
to multiple  new programs that have been successful in attracting consumers to  downtown

MEETING: City Council Meeting - Agenda Only | DATE: 2020-11-23 | CLIP_ID: 774 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 774

   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
November 23, 2020 
 
 The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:06 p.m. via electronic conference meeting .  Attending electronic conference meeting  were: 
MAYOR - Marian Orr, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete 
Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White. Absent: Scott Roybal.  Also 
attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk. The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. )  Upon request by Mr . White, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Cook and Mr. Luna, agenda item #17(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda. 
Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received by Dr. Rinne and Mr. White, 
agenda item  #19(b) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Luna moved to approve  
remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present.  
 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on November 9, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter 10.44, Weight Limits, of Title 10, 
Vehicles and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. (Postponed from 
November 9, 2020). Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, expressed appreciation to all parties 
involved with their assistance on the ordinance and reported the amendment has been mutually 
agreed upon by city  staff and the trucking industr y.  Tom Cobb,  City Engineer, advised the 
ordinance was a collaborative effort and voiced appreciation to Chief Kozak for working with the 
trucking industry.  Mayor Orr expressed appreciation to members of city staff and other entities 
for their work.  Sheila Foertsch, Wyoming Trucking Association, thanked all parties involved for 
the additional work and time spent on the ordinance.  Ms. Foertsch advised she is comfortable with 
the amendment and believes it’s a good product  of compromise by all parties.  Mayor Orr 
referenced comments made via the chat function in Zoom in support of the ordinance.  Rick Fierro, 
Ram Trucking, thanked Mr. Cobb and Chief Kozak,  noting a lot of time was  spent working to 
refine the ordinance and advised the finished product will be conducive for everyone.   Reporting 
for Public Services Committee, Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. 
Luna.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by substitute dated November 23, 2020, seconded by Mr. Cook.  
Dr. Rinne explained the amendment is the final product of all of the meetings and discussion that 
has taken place between Chief Kozak and  the trucking industry.  Upon inquiry, Joe Chenchar, 
Assistant City Attorney, advised Sc hedules H & I were submitted  and approved by him earlier 
today.  Upon inquiry, Kris Jones, City Clerk, explained the schedules are part of the ordinance and 
   
included in the substitute and do not require a separate motion to approve.  Mr. Chenchar explained 
the schedules are incorporated by reference in the ordinance text.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing bod y present.  Concerns were voic ed regarding timing of 
implementing the ordinance amendment and that due to the Covid-19 Pandemic, small businesses 
are struggling who participate in the chain of services , noting it could be devastating to them.  
Comments were made commending the work product and collaboration between city staff and 
industry partners and additional information was provided on previous comments made in support 
of the ordinance by members of city staff and the trucking industry.  Additional comments were 
made related to the ordinance amendment not being affected by the Covid-19 Pandemic and that 
the ordinance requires truckers to abide by state and federal laws when within city limits and that 
any time is a good time to implement.  Discussion included opposition to adding new restricted 
routes within the city that didn’t exist before.  It was further noted that the trucking industry spoke 
in favor of the ordinance amendments.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present with exception of Mr. Case and Mr. Shanor voting “no”.        
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Section 2.84.070, Board of Adjustment, of 
Chapter 2.84, Miscellaneous Boards and Commissions, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, 
of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to correct cross references to the Board 
of Adjustment.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Section 15.52.060(B), Notification of 
Issuance or Denial -Appeal, and Section 15.52.100 , Variances, of Chapter 15.52, Solar Access 
Permit System, of Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Municipal Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, to correct cross references to the Board of Adjustment.  Mr. Cook moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Establishing a Planned Unit Development (PUD) District 
for property generally located northwest of the intersection of North Gate Avenue and Yellowstone 
Road.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr . Luna.  Upon inquiry, 
Charles Bloom, Planning and Development Director, advised there was discussion by the Planning 
Commission related to concern with a sunken garden within the development that may not be ADA 
compatible.  C asey Palma, Stile Surveying Services, agent for owner, reported a sunken garden 
was designed that will also serve as a detention pond and the owner opted for a walkway to look 
down on the area.  Mr. Palma advised they have and are still looking for possibilities to provide 
an ADA pathway over the top.  He further noted the street  has over a 5% grade and that a letter 
has been submitted by the project architect related to ADA accessibility, noting ADA compliance 
is not required because it is private and not accessible by the general public.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” - all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing the City Treasurer to discharge uncollectible debts in the 
amount of $39,348.39 for City of Cheyenne accounts and $23,290.93 for Board of Public Utilities 
sanitation accounts pursuant to Wyoming Statute §16 -4-502.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#6102) 
   
[CA] RESOLUTION – Approving water and sanitary sewer services, by Outside User Water 
and Sanitary Sewer Services Agreements, for Tri-State Generation and Transmission, Lot 4, Block 
10, North Range Business Park, 3 rd Filing, situated in the NE¼ SE¼ of Section 4,  Township 13, 
North, Range 67 West, of the 6 th P.M., Laramie County.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
(#6103) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – To rededicate unknown funds and unused donations from various 
accounts within the City of Cheyenne’s Agency Fund – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor 
– Mr. Luna)   
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Repealing a portion of Resolution No. 5951 which pertains to the 
dedication of General Fund Reserves to design and build a second level on the Cheyenne 
Municipal Court Building – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Luna) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Certifying CONNECT 2045:  Update to the Master Transportation and 
Future Land Use Plan of PlanCheyenne for the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Urban Area – 
referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
[CA] RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a final plat for The 
Enclave at Northgate, a replat of a portion of Lots 6 and 7, all of Lot 8, and the east half of Lots 9 
thru 12, Block 19, North Cheyen ne 2 nd Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming 
(located northwest of the intersection of North Gate Avenue and Yellowstone Road) – referred to 
Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
[CA] Preliminary Plat for Sweetgrass, 5th Filing, a subdivision of a portion of the N ½ of Section 
16, Township 13 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located south 
of E. College Drive, east of Sweetgrass Drive) .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried 
(acknowledged Planning Commission’s recommendation to approve). 
 
Memorandum of Understanding between EQ Capital , LLC, and the City of Cheyenne to 
build a technology ecosystem in Wyoming focused on investing in tech startups. (Postponed from 
November 9, 2020).   Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Mr. White explained 
city staff worked to come up with an amendment to address concerns.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Jones 
confirmed that Mr. Laybourn cast an affirmative vote at the November 16 th Finance Committee 
meeting, therefore, the agenda item met Consent Agenda criteria.  Mr. White moved to amend by 
substitute dated November 20, 2020, seconded by Mr . Luna.  Appreciation was voiced for the 
amendment and work by Brendan Ames, Chief Economic Development Officer, Attorney’s office 
and EQ Capital, noting the amendment removes previous concerns and provides for mutual official 
recognition that the parties we will work together on the project.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body  present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting 
“no”.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present 
with exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. (#7259)   
 
 
   
[CA] Development and Maintenance Agreement between Cheyenne-Laramie County 
Corporation for Economic Development, the City of Cheyenne, and the Cheyenne Business Park 
Owners Association for the Cheyenne Business Parkway Corridor Plan .  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. (#7260) 
 
[CA] Grant agreement between Memorial Hospital of Laramie County d/b/a Cheyenne Regional 
Medical Center and the City of Cheyenne Police Department for Law Enforcement Assisted 
Diversion (LEAD) Program officer overtime and client support (CRMC LEAD Grant) – referred 
to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7221  between the City of Cheyenne and 
Containment Systems for additional services to complete the Expansion of Landfill Permanent 
Litter Fence Project (Solid Waste Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not 
to exceed $17,435.00). (#7221) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #3 to Contract #5932 between the City of Cheyenne and Solid Waste 
Professionals of Wyoming, for Happy Jack Landfill Expansion to extend the contract to March 31, 
2022 (Solid Waste Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5932) 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract # 7216 between Memorial Hospital of Laramie 
County d/b/a Cheyenne Regional Medical Center and the City of Cheyenne Police Department for 
Law Enforcement Assisted Diversion (LEAD) Program (CRMC LEAD Grant) – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7117 between the City of Cheyenne and Western 
Research and Development, Ltd. for additional  design services for the 2020 Signal Upgrade 
Design Services Contract (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] Consideration of Bid # S-2-21 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Casper 
Electric, Inc., for the 24 th / 25th / Carey / Pioneer Traffic Signal Upgrade Project (2015-2018 1% 
Sales Tax) .  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid in an amount not to exceed 
$677,384.84). (#7261) 
 
 Consideration of Bid #S-8-21 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and JTL Group 
Inc. d/b/a Knife River, for the 2020 Miscellaneous Concrete Project (2015-2018 & 2019-2022 1% 
Sales Tax).  Mr. Luna moved to accept the bid in an amount not to exceed $443,436.48, seconded 
by Mr. White . Upon inquiry, Tom Cobb, City Engineer, provided information on locations of 
concrete work to be completed through th e bid.  Mr. Cobb reported this phase of the project will 
exhaust approximately half of available project funding and that the other half wi ll be expended 
through a future bid for remaining locations planned to be improved.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#7262) 
 
 No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
   
 Upon inquiry by Mayor Orr, Robin Lockman, City Treasurer, provided information on the 
September 2020 sales tax collection explaining  she received an email from  the Department of 
Revenue that additional revenue was from the Roundhouse Windmill Project in an approximate 
amount of $2 million dollars.  She further reported that current 6th Penny Sales Tax collection is 
predicted to be complete by July of 2021, however it could be sooner if sales tax income remains 
strong.  Mayor Orr urged everyone to get together in January to start discussions on  future 6th 
Penny Sales Tax projects  and acknowledged that while it seems like a long way away , a lot of 
planning remains to be done.  No further comments were made related to the Revenue Report as 
presented. 
 
 Announcement of a Public Hearing was made to be held December 14, 2020 at 6:00 p.m. 
via electr onic conference meeting, to determine whether compliance of annexation conditions 
required by Wyoming Statute §15 -1-402 exist for land located southeast of the intersection of 
Powderhouse Road and Carlson Street.   
  
  There bein g no fur ther business to co me before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:40 p.m.  
 
 
 
        Submitted by, 
       
 
        Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk
